Pointwise estimates for the fundamental solutions of higher order Schrödinger equations in odd dimensions II: high dimensional case
Han Cheng, Shanlin Huang, Tianxiao Huang, Quan Zheng

Abstract
. In this paper, for any odd n n and any integer m ≥ 1 m\geq 1 with n > 4 ​ m n>4m , we study the fundamental solution of the higher order Schrödinger equation i ​ ∂ t u ⁡ ( x , t ) = ( ( − Δ ) m + V ⁡ ( x ) ) ​ u ​ ( x , t ) , t ∈ ℝ , x ∈ ℝ n , {\rm i}\partial_{t}u(x,t)=((-\Delta)^{m}+V(x))u(x,t),\quad t\in\mathbb{R},\,\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}, where V V is a real-valued C n + 1 2 − 2 ​ m C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m} potential with certain decay. Let P a ​ c ​ ( H ) P_{ac}(H) denote the projection onto the absolutely continuous spectrum space of H = ( − Δ ) m + V H=(-\Delta)^{m}+V , and assume that H H has no positive embedded eigenvalue. Our main result says that e − i ​ t ​ H ​ P a ​ c ​ ( H ) e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H) has integral kernel K ⁡ ( t , x , y ) K(t,x,y) satisfying | K ( t , x , y ) | ≤ C ( 1 + | t | ) − ( n 2 ​ m − σ ) ( 1 + | t | − n 2 ​ m ) ( 1 + | t | − 1 2 ​ m | x − y | ) − n ⁡ ( m − 1 ) 2 ​ m − 1 , t ≠ 0 , x , y ∈ ℝ n , |K(t,x,y)|\leq C(1+|t|)^{-(\frac{n}{2m}-\sigma)}(1+|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}})\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad t\neq 0,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}, where σ = 2 \sigma=2 if 0 0 is an eigenvalue of H H , and σ = 0 \sigma=0 otherwise. A similar result for smoothing operators H α 2 ​ m ​ e − i ​ t ​ H ​ P a ​ c ​ ( H ) H^{\frac{\alpha}{2m}}e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H) is also given. The regularity condition V ∈ C n + 1 2 − 2 ​ m V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m} is optimal in the second order case, and it also seems optimal when m > 1 m>1 .



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
35A08, 81Q10

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Motivation and main result

We continue the study in
[
2
]
for the pointwise estimate for the fundamental solution of higher order Schrödinger equation

{\rm i}\partial_{t}u(x,t)=((-\Delta)^{m}+V(x))u(x,t),\quad t\in\mathbb{R},\,\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(1.1)

where
m
is any positive integer, and
V
is a real-valued decaying potential in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

In the first paper
[
2
]
of this series, it has been proved when
n<4m
and
n
is odd that, if
V
has sufficient decay at the infinity, denoted by
P_{ac}(H)
the projection onto the absolutely continuous spectrum space of
H=(-\Delta)^{m}+V
, then
e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)
has integral kernel
K(t,x,y)
satisfying

|K(t,x,y)|\leq C(1+|t|)^{-h}(1+|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}})\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad t\neq 0,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(1.2)

where
h>0
can be specified by
m,n
and the zero energy resonances of
H
. The motivation of considering such problem was closely related to the dispersive estimates of Schrödinger equations, for instance, we refer to the classical works
[
18
,
25
,
21
]
and the survey papers
[
22
,
23
]
. Moreover, it was also inspired by many recent studies on higher order Schrödinger equations with potentials (see e.g. in
[
10
,
11
,
6
,
4
,
7
,
9
,
8
,
3
,
12
,
16
,
14
,
20
]
). The overall strategy to show (
1.2
) was to first decompose
e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)
into low and high energy parts by the spectral theorem

\begin{split}e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)&=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda}\chi(\lambda){\rm d}E_{\lambda}+\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda){\rm d}E_{\lambda}\\
&:=e^{-{\rm i}tH}\chi(H)P_{ac}(H)+e^{-{\rm i}tH}\tilde{\chi}(H)P_{ac}(H),\end{split}

(1.3)

where
{\rm d}E_{\lambda}
is the spectral measure of
H
,
\chi(\lambda)+\tilde{\chi}(\lambda)\equiv 1
, and
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}((-\lambda_{0}^{\frac{1}{2m}},\lambda_{0}^{\frac{1}{2m}}))
for sufficiently small
\lambda_{0}>0
. The difficulty of proving (
1.2
) mostly lies in the kernel estimate of the low energy part
e^{-{\rm i}tH}\chi(H)P_{ac}(H)
, where the asymptotic expansion of
{\rm d}E_{\lambda}
as
\lambda\rightarrow 0
was obtained in a detailed form. It turns out that in low odd dimensions
n<4m
, the asymptotic expansion of
{\rm d}E_{\lambda}
depends on the resonance type of
H
at zero energy, which finally leads to the dependence
h
in (
1.2
). We refer to
[
2
]
for the definition of zero energy resonance.

The current paper deals with such problem in high odd dimensions
n>4m
. In contrary to the low dimensional case, we shall see that when
n>4m
, the low energy part
e^{-{\rm i}tH}\chi(H)P_{ac}(H)
is somehow easier to deal with for there is no nontrivial zero energy resonance (i.e.
0
is either regular or an eigenvalue of
H
). However, the high energy part becomes quite complicated where the regularity of
V
will be involved. Our main result is the following.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
n
be odd,
m\geq 1
be an integer with
n>4m
,
V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be real-valued satisfying
|V(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-(n+2)-}
and

|\partial^{\alpha}V(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-(\frac{3n+1}{2}-2m)-},\quad 0\leq|\alpha|\leq\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}.

(1.4)

We also assume that
H=(-\Delta)^{m}+V
has no positive embedded eigenvalue. Then
e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)
has integral kernel
K(t,x,y)
with

(i) If the zero energy of
H
is regular, i.e.
0
is not an eigenvalue of
H
, then

|K(t,x,y)|\leq C|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad t\neq 0,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(1.5)

(ii) If
0
is an eigenvalue of
H
, then

|K(t,x,y)|\leq C(1+|t|)^{-(\frac{n}{2m}-2)}(1+|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}})\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad t\neq 0,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(1.6)

We remark that the distinction by the zero energy of
H
is completely due to the estimate of the low energy part
e^{-{\rm i}tH}\chi(H)P_{ac}(H)
where (
1.4
) will not be used actually. When the zero energy of
H
is regular, the estimate (
1.5
) coincides with the free case
V\equiv 0
(see
[
19
]
), and it immediately implies the dispersive bound

\|e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)\|_{L^{1}-L^{\infty}}\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}},

(1.7)

but the converse is not true if
m>1
. We also note that when
0
is an eigenvalue of
H
, the estimate (
1.6
) implies the dispersive bound

\|e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)\|_{L^{1}-L^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-(\frac{n}{2m}-2)}(1+|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}),

and this coincides with the result in Goldberg-Green
[
13
]
for the second order case
m=1
where better long time decay was also discussed if the zero eigenspace has more vanishing structure.

The main focus of this paper is to show how the regularity of
V
plays a role in the estimate of the high energy part
e^{-{\rm i}tH}\tilde{\chi}(H)P_{ac}(H)
, which leads to the assumptions
V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and (
1.4
) in Theorem
1.1
. The fact that in dimensions
n>4m
, the regularity of
V
may be needed was first found in the second order case
m=1
, i.e. the dispersive estimate of Schrödinger equations:

\|e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)\|_{L^{1}-L^{\infty}}\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2}},\quad H=-\Delta+V.

(1.8)

In Journé-Soffer-Sogge
[
18
]
, the authors established for the first time the dispersive estimate (
1.8
) for
n\geq 3
under the regularity requirement
\hat{V}\in L^{1}
. Here
\hat{V}
represents the Fourier transform of
V
. In fact, the regularity of
V
is necessary when
n>3
, because Goldberg-Visan
[
15
]
has shown that there exists compactly supported real-valued
V\in C^{\frac{n-3}{2}-}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that estimate (
1.8
) fails, while Erdoǧan-Green
[
5
]
later proved that (
1.8
) holds with
C^{\frac{n-3}{2}}
decaying real-valued potentials when
n=5,7
(assuming zero energy of
H
is regular). It seems that when
n\geq 9
is odd, the dispersive estimate (
1.8
) with
C^{\frac{n-3}{2}}
potential is still not known, but Erdoǧan-Green
[
5
]
gives a brief technical discussion for the possibility and difficulty of proving such result.

In the higher order case
m>1
, when
n>4m
and
n
is odd, to the authors’ best knowledge, the only existing works Erdoǧan-Green
[
6
,
7
]
that are able to obtain the dispersive bound (
1.7
) have to use the
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
boundedness of the wave operators, where the regularity condition on
V
were put in the form

\|\mathscr{F}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\sigma}V(\cdot))\|_{L^{\frac{n-1-\delta}{n-2m-\delta}}}\lesssim 1,\quad\sigma>\mbox{$\frac{2n-4m}{n-1-\delta}+\delta$},\,0<\delta<<1,

(1.9)

which roughly means that
V
is required to have more than
\frac{n}{n-1}(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)
reasonable derivatives. We mention that the study of
L^{p}
boundedness of the wave operators is technically quite similar to the study of dispersive estimate, see
[
8
,
14
,
20
,
12
]
for example.

It seems not clear now what regularity condition should be the sharp one to imply dispersive estimate (
1.7
), however when
n>4m
, Erdoǧan-Goldberg-Green
[
3
]
has constructed compactly supported
V\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for every
\alpha\in[0,\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)
such that the wave operators for
H=(-\Delta)^{m}+V
are not bounded on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and this may be seen as a circumstantial evidence that indicates the sharpness of our assumption
V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
in the even stronger result Theorem
1.1
(in high odd dimensions) than the dispersive estimate.

As discussed in
[
2
]
, the immediate applications of Theorem
1.1
and its proof are the
L^{p}-L^{q}
type estimate and smoothing estimate for
e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)
, and we only phrase them without repeating the proofs.

Proposition 1.2
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.1
, we have the following.

(i)
It follows that

\|e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)\|_{L^{p}_{*}-L^{q}_{*}}\lesssim\begin{cases}|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})},\quad&\text{if $0$ is not an eigenvalue of $H$},\\
(1+|t|)^{2(\frac{2}{p}-1)}|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})},\quad&\text{if $0$ is an eigenvalue of $H$},\end{cases}

where

L^{p}_{*}-L^{q}_{*}=\begin{cases}L^{1}-L^{\tau_{m},\infty}\,\text{or}\,H^{1}-L^{\tau_{m}},&\text{if}\,\,(p,q)=(1,\tau_{m}),\\[4.0pt]
L^{\tau^{\prime}_{m},1}-L^{\infty}\,\text{or}\,L^{\tau_{m}^{\prime}}-\text{BMO},&\text{if}\,\,(p,q)=(\tau^{\prime}_{m},\infty),\\[4.0pt]
L^{p}-L^{q},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\tau_{m}=\frac{2m-1}{m-1}
,
\tau_{m}^{\prime}=\frac{2m-1}{m}
,
H^{1}
is the Hardy space on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, BMO is the space of functions with bounded mean oscillation on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
(\frac{1}{q},\frac{1}{q})
lies in the closed quadrilateral ABCD explained in Figure
1
.

(ii)
If
\alpha\in[0,n(m-1)]
, denoted by
K_{\alpha}(t,x,y)
the kernel of
H^{\frac{\alpha}{2m}}e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)
, then

\begin{split}&|K_{\alpha}(t,x,y)|\\
\lesssim&\begin{cases}|t|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)-\alpha}{2m-1}},\quad&\text{if $0$ is not an eigenvalue of $H$,}\\
(1+|t|)^{-\frac{1+\alpha}{2m}}(1+|t|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2m}})\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)-\alpha}{2m-1}},\quad&\text{if $0$ is an eigenvalue of $H$.}\end{cases}\end{split}

\frac{1}{q}
\frac{1}{p}
D=(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}^{\prime}},0)
C=(1,0)
1
B=(1,\frac{1}{\tau_{m}})
(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})

Figure 1
.
The
L^{p}-L^{q}
estimates

1.2.
Technical difficulties in dimensions
n>4m

In Erdoǧan-Green
[
5
]
for the dispersive estimate with
C^{\frac{n-3}{2}}
potentials in the case
m=1
and
n=5,7
, the authors pioneered a delicate integration by parts scheme, where sophisticated singularities are involved. For a comparison, we shortly discuss the counterpart in this paper which develops the ideas in
[
5
]
for
m\geq 1
and all odd
n>4m
.

We will encounter an integration by parts scheme for some specific oscillatory integrals, where a special case loosely speaking appears in the following form

\lambda^{k(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda(|x_{0}-x_{1}|+\cdots+|x_{k}-x_{k+1}|)}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i=0}^{k}|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i=1}^{k}V(x_{i})$}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},

(1.10)

where
k
is any fixed positive integer,
x_{0},x_{k+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are fixed parameters,
V
has certain regularity and decay, and
\lambda^{k(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}
comes from the resolvent expansion where
k
many free resolvents
R^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})=\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}((-\Delta)^{m}-\lambda^{2m}\mp{\rm i}0)
are involved. In the kernel of each resolvent (see (
2.1
)), there is a term with the highest power in
\lambda
presented as a finite sum of

\lambda^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}\frac{e^{{\rm i}e^{{\rm i}\frac{\pi k}{m}}\lambda|x-y|}}{|x-y|^{\frac{n-1}{2}}}

which provides the growth
\lambda^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}
. For our final purpose of fundamental solution estimate, we need to eliminate the total growth
\lambda^{k(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}
, and a natural choice is to integrate by parts
k(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)
times in (
1.10
). Averagely, we have to perform integration by parts
\frac{n+1}{2}-2m
times in each
x_{i}
variable, so
V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}
seems to be a proper condition.

However, integration by parts may introduce complicated singularities. Denoted by
E_{x_{i-1}x_{i}x_{i}x_{i+1}}=\frac{x_{i-1}-x_{i}}{|x_{i-1}-x_{i}|}-\frac{x_{i}-x_{i+1}}{|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|}
, note that

\nabla_{x_{i}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda(|x_{0}-x_{1}|+\cdots+|x_{k}-x_{k+1}|)}=-{\rm i}\lambda e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda(|x_{0}-x_{1}|+\cdots+|x_{k}-x_{k+1}|)}E_{x_{i-1}x_{i}x_{i}x_{i+1}},

(1.11)

then integration by parts in
x_{i}
naturally introduces a type of singularity at the zeros of
E_{x_{i-1}x_{i}x_{i}x_{i+1}}
, that is when
x_{i}
lies in the line segment
[x_{i-1},x_{i+1}]
, and we will call
|E_{x_{i-1}x_{i}x_{i}x_{i+1}}|^{-1}
a line singularity. It is not hard to show

\begin{split}\nabla_{x_{i}}|E_{x_{i-1}x_{i}x_{i}x_{i+1}}|^{-1}=|E_{x_{i-1}x_{i}x_{i}x_{i+1}}|^{-2}O\left(|x_{i-1}-x_{i}|^{-1}+|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|^{-1}\right),\\
\nabla_{x_{i}}|x_{i-1}-x_{i}|^{-1}=O\left(|x_{i-1}-x_{i}|^{-2}\right),\quad\nabla_{x_{i}}|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|^{-1}=O\left(|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|^{-2}\right),\end{split}

(1.12)

then we see integration by parts in
x_{i}
each time introduces terms with at most two more line singularities
|E_{x_{i-1}x_{i}x_{i}x_{i+1}}|^{-2}
, and one more point singularity
|x_{i-1}-x_{i}|^{-1}
or
|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|^{-1}
. The more complicated issue is that, if we further integrate by parts in
x_{i+1}
, not only the new line singularity
|E_{x_{i}x_{i+1}x_{i+1}x_{i+2}}|^{-1}
will be introduced, but differentiation in
x_{i+1}
will also drop on and increase the previous line singularity
|E_{x_{i-1}x_{i}x_{i}x_{i+1}}|^{-1}
and point singularity
|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|^{-1}
.

Therefore, the way we perform integration by parts leads to estimates of integrals with some mixtures of line and point singularities, and finally, it is important to consider what types of such mixtures are integrable. In Erdoǧan-Green
[
5
]
, for the case of
m=1
,
n=5
, the problem was reduced to consider integrals with two different types of line singularities in the form

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{-3-}{\rm d}z}{|x-z|^{k}|x-y|^{l}|E_{xzzw}|^{n-3}|E_{zwwy}|^{n-3}},

and in the case of
m=1
,
n=7
, they have to finally consider integrals with three different types of line singularities in the form

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{-3-}\langle w\rangle^{-3-}{\rm d}z{\rm d}w}{|x-z|^{k}|z-w|^{l}|w-y|^{p}|E_{xzzw}|^{n-3}|E_{zwwy}|^{n-3}|E_{zwyu}|^{n-3}}.

It was also expected in
[
5
]
that in the general case of
m=1
, odd
n\geq 9
, the similar consideration will lead to estimates for integrals which involve many different types of line singularities, but the analysis of such integrals should be quite different and more complicated.

In this paper for the much more general case
m\geq 1
and odd
n>4m
, what meets the expectation in
[
5
]
is that, we do have to consider integrals with many different types of line singularities in the form of

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}\cdots\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}}{|x_{0}-x_{1}|^{a_{0}}\cdots|x_{k}-x_{k+1}|^{a_{k}}}|E_{\eta_{1},1}|^{-q_{1}}\cdots|E_{\eta_{k},k}|^{-q_{k}}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},

(1.13)

where
E_{\eta_{i},i}=E_{x_{\eta_{i}}x_{\eta_{i}-1}x_{i}x_{i+1}}
. Somewhat unexpected in
[
5
]
however, we will reduce the estimate of (
1.13
) to estimating integrals with only two different types of line singularities in the form

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle|x-y|^{-k_{1}}\rangle\langle|y-z|^{-l_{1}}\rangle\langle y-y_{0}\rangle^{-\beta-}}{\langle x-y\rangle^{k_{2}}\langle y-z\rangle^{l_{2}}|E_{xyyz}|^{p}|E_{ww^{\prime}xy}|^{q}}{\rm d}y,

(1.14)

and this is due to the crucial fact that our strategy of performing integration by parts will give a specific structure of the indices
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
in (
1.13
), which we will call ”admissible” in Definition
5.10
, and the admissibility of
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
allows us to always find a specific variable
x_{\tau}
, such that a successful estimate of the integral in
x_{\tau}
by (
1.14
) will preserve the form of (
1.13
).

1.3.
Plan of the paper

We outline the strategy for proving Theorem
1.1
.
Our starting point is the Stone’s formula

\langle e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)f,\,g\rangle=\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda}\langle(R^{+}(\lambda)-R^{-}(\lambda))f,g\rangle{\rm d}\lambda,\quad f,g\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

(1.15)

where
R^{\pm}(\lambda):=(H-\lambda\mp{\rm i}0)^{-1}
. The assumption that
H
has no positive eigenvalue allows us to split

\displaystyle e^{-{\rm i}tH}P_{ac}(H)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda}\left(R^{+}(\lambda)-R^{-}(\lambda)\right)(\chi(\lambda)+\tilde{\chi}(\lambda))\,{\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle:=e^{-{\rm i}tH}\chi(H)P_{ac}(H)+e^{-{\rm i}tH}\tilde{\chi}(H)P_{ac}(H),

into low and high energy part, where
\chi(\lambda)+\tilde{\chi}(\lambda)\equiv 1
,
\chi\in C_{0}^{\infty}((-\lambda_{0}^{\frac{1}{2m}},\lambda_{0}^{\frac{1}{2m}}))
some
\lambda_{0}>0
, and
\chi(\lambda)=1
when
\lambda\in((-(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2})^{\frac{1}{2m}},(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2})^{\frac{1}{2m}}))
. Theorem
1.1
follows immediately from the two theorems below.

Theorem
1.1
(low energy part)
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.1
, if
\lambda_{0}\in(0,1)
is small enough, then
e^{-{\rm i}tH}\chi(H)P_{ac}(H)
has integral kernel
K_{L}(t,x,y)
satisfying

\left|K_{L}(t,x,y)\right|\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-(\frac{n}{2m}-\sigma)}(1+|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}})\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad t\neq 0,\,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

where
\sigma=2
if
0
is an eigenvalue of
H
, and
\sigma=0
otherwise.

Theorem
1.1
(high energy part)
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.1
,
e^{-{\rm i}tH}\tilde{\chi}(H)P_{ac}(H)
(for any fixed
\lambda_{0}>0
) has integral kernel
K_{H}(t,x,y)
satisfying

\left|K_{H}(t,x,y)\right|\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad t\neq 0,\,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

In Section
2
, we give some technical preliminaries, including the kernels of the boundary values of the free resolvents
R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})=((-\Delta)^{m}-\lambda^{2m}\mp{\rm i}0)^{-1}
, the expansions of the perturbed resolvents
R^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})=(H-\lambda^{2m}\mp{\rm i}0)^{-1}
, estimates for integrals with point and line singularities like (
1.14
), and two oscillatory integral estimates in one dimension.

In Section
3
, we prove the low energy part of Theorem
1.1
. We first decompose

e^{-{\rm i}tH}\chi(H)P_{ac}(H)=\sum_{k=0}^{2N}\Omega^{low}_{k}-\left(\Omega^{+,low}_{r}-\Omega^{-,low}_{r}\right)

via spectral theorem and an iteration of the resolvent identity. The estimate for
\Omega^{low}_{k}(t,x,y)
is given in Section
3.1
. The estimate for the remainder term

(\Omega^{+,low}_{r}-\Omega^{-,low}_{r})(t,x,y)
is given in Section
3.2
. We note that, in contrast to the situation when
n<4m
, the
asymptotic expansions of
(U+vR_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})v)^{-1}
(
v(x)=|V(x)|^{\frac{1}{2}},\,\,U=\sgn V
) for small
\lambda>0
are much simpler in the case
n>4m
, which has been treated in
[
11
]
(see Theorem
2.1
).

In Section
4
, we prove the high energy part of Theorem
1.1
except for a particularly complicated part. Similar to the low energy part, we first decompose the high energy part
e^{-{\rm i}tH}\tilde{\chi}(H)P_{ac}(H)
, by a slightly different resolvent identity of
R^{\pm}(\lambda)
, into

e^{-\mathrm{i}tH}P_{ac}(H)\tilde{\chi}(H)=\sum_{k=0}^{2K-1}\Omega_{k}^{high}+\Omega_{K,r}^{+,high}-\Omega_{K,r}^{-,high}.

The estimates for
\Omega_{K,r}^{\pm,high}(t,x,y)
can be proved if
K
is chosen sufficiently large as already shown in
[
2
]
, so we omit the proof.
\Omega_{k}^{high}(t,x,y)
can be written as the oscillatory integral

\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{\mathrm{i}t\lambda}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\left(\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i=0}^{k}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda)(x_{i}-x_{i+1})-\prod\limits_{i=0}^{k}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda)(x_{i}-x_{i+1})$}\right)\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i=1}^{k}V(x_{i})$}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}\right){\rm d}\lambda,

where
R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda)(x_{i}-x_{i+1})
are the kernels of
R_{0}^{\pm}
(
\lambda
), and we will further decompose
\Omega_{k}^{high}(t,x,y)
into
\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)
and
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
by the space-time regions
\{X\leq\delta T\}
and
\{X\geq\delta T\}
respectively for some small
\delta>0
, where
X=|x-x_{1}|+|x_{1}-x_{2}|+\cdots+|x_{k}-y|
,
T=|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}}+|t|
. The estimate of
\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)
is given in Section
4.2
, but the estimates of
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
is only sketched in Section
4.3
, leaving the technical details for a particularly complicated part in Section
5
.

In Section
5
, we complete the estimate for
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
, where the techniques for solving the issues indicated in Section
1.2
are fully demonstrated. We first heuristically discuss in Section
5.1
the overall plan of treating a particular type of terms
I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)
in the expansion of
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
where the regularity of
V
is needed. In Section
5.2
, we establish an integration by parts scheme for integrals in a more general form than (
1.10
), and the main conclusion is Proposition
5.12
, where after integration by parts, a bunch of clusters of line singularities
\prod_{i=1}^{s}\|F_{i}\|^{-{p_{i}}}
shows up in the integrand. Here
F_{i}
is a particular set of line singularities
\{E_{x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{1}+1}x_{j_{1}}x_{j_{1}+1}},\cdots,E_{x_{i_{r}}x_{i_{r}+1}x_{j_{r}}x_{j_{r}+1}}\}
, and

\|F_{i}\|=\left(\sum_{l=1}^{r}|E_{x_{i_{l}}x_{i_{l}+1}x_{j_{l}}x_{j_{l}+1}}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In Section
5.3
, with a specific structure of these
F_{i}
that comes along with our integration by parts scheme, we turn to reduce the family of
\|F_{i}\|
, so that what have to be estimated will be reduced to integrals in the form of (
1.13
) in Section
5.4
, where we can take the advantage of the admissibility of
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
mentioned in Section
1.2
to successfully complete all relevant estimates by only applying estimate for integrals like (
1.14
) with two different types of line singularities, and the main conclusion is Proposition
5.19
. With all the technical preparation above, we finally complete the estimate for
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
in Section
5.5
.

1.4.
Notations

We first collect some common notations and conventions.
Throughout the paper,
\mathbb{N}_{+}=\{1,2,\ldots\}
,
\mathbb{N}_{0}=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
, and
\mathbb{Z}=\{0,\pm 1,\pm 2,\ldots\}
,
L^{2}=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{C})
.
[l]
denotes the greatest integer at most
l
.
A\lesssim B
means
A\leq CB
, where
C>0
is an absolute constant whose dependence will be specified whenever necessary, and the value of C may vary from line to line.
a-
(resp.
a+
) means
a-\epsilon
(resp.
a+\epsilon
) for some
\epsilon>0
.

Next, we define some (vector-valued) function classes that will be used in most of the oscillatory integral estimates in this paper.

Definition 1.3
.

For
b\in\mathbb{R}
,
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and an open set
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}
.

(i)
We say
f\in S_{K}^{b}(\Omega)
if
f(\lambda)\in C^{K}(\Omega)
and

|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}f(\lambda)|\lesssim|\lambda|^{b-j},\quad\lambda\in\Omega,\,\,0\leq j\leq K.

(1.16)

We also denote

S^{b}(\Omega)=\bigcap_{K=0}^{\infty}S_{K}^{b}(\Omega).

(1.17)

(ii)

For functions with parameters in the following form, we say

f\left(\lambda,x,y,s_{1},s_{2}\right)\in S_{K}^{b}(\Omega)
, if
\lambda\mapsto f\left(\lambda,x,y,s_{1},s_{2}\right)
is in
C^{K}(\Omega)
for fixed
x,y,s_{1},s_{2}
, and

|\partial_{\lambda}^{\gamma}f\left(\lambda,x,y,s_{1},s_{2}\right)|\lesssim\lambda^{b-\gamma},\quad\lambda\in\Omega,\,\,\,0\leq j\leq K,

(1.18)

holds uniformly with respect to the parameters
x,y,s_{1},s_{2}
.

(iii)

We say
g(\lambda,s,\cdot,x)\in S_{K}^{b}(\Omega,\,\,\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}})
, if

\lambda\mapsto g(\lambda,s,y,x)
is in
C^{K}(\Omega)
for any fixed
s,x,y
, and

\|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}g(\lambda,s,\cdot,x)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{j}|\lambda|^{b-j},\quad\lambda\in\Omega,\,\,\,0\leq j\leq K

(1.19)

and
C_{j}>0
does not depend on the parameters
x,s
.

(iv)

We say
T(\lambda)\in\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{b}(\Omega)
if
\{T(\lambda)\}_{\lambda\in\Omega}
is a family of bounded operators in
L^{2}
such that

|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\langle T(\lambda)f,g\rangle|\leq C_{j}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}}|\lambda|^{b-j},\quad\lambda\in\Omega,\,\,\,0\leq j\leq K

(1.20)

holds for all
f,g\in L^{2}
, and the constant
C_{j}
is independent of
f,g,\lambda
.

We mention in particular that in Section
5
, there are some frequently used notations which are not standard, and we make an index here for readers who go into those details.

•

E_{xyyz}
,
E_{w^{\prime}wxy}
(See (
2.5
)),
E_{i,j}
(see (
5.10
)),
\|F\|
(see (
5.11
));

•

L_{i}
(see (
5.6
));

•

N(A,i),\,L(A,i)
(see (
5.7
)),
D_{i}A
(see (
5.8
)),
D_{I}A
(see (
5.9
));

•

\iota_{j}
(see (
5.56
)).

2.
Preliminaries

In this section, we gather some technical preliminaries that shall be used through the paper.

2.1.
The free resolvents

For
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus[0,\infty)
, we set
R_{0}(z)=((-\Delta)^{m}-z)^{-1}
. For
\lambda>0
, the well known limiting absorption principle (see
[
1
]
) implies that the weak* limits
R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m}):=R_{0}(\lambda^{2m}\pm i0)=w*-\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}R_{0}(\lambda^{2m}\pm{\rm i}\epsilon)
exist as bounded operators between certain weighted
L^{2}
spaces. When
n\geq 4m
is odd, it was shown in
[
2
]
that if
\lambda>0
, the convolution kernels of
R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})
satisfy

R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})(x)=\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}m\lambda^{2m}}\sum_{k\in I^{\pm}}\frac{\lambda_{k}^{2}e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{k}|x|}}{|x|^{n-2}}\sum^{\frac{n-3}{2}}_{j=0}{c_{j}({\rm i}\lambda_{k}|x|)^{j}},\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\,\mbox{$c_{j}=\frac{(-2)^{j}(n-3-j)!}{j!(\frac{n-3}{2}-j)!}$},

(2.1)

where
\lambda_{k}=\lambda e^{{\rm i}\frac{\pi k}{m}}
,
I^{+}=\{0,1,\cdots,m-1\}
and
I^{-}=\{1,\cdots,m\}
. We will also need the following facts which are special cases of
[
2
, Proposition 2.1]
:

R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(x)-R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(x)=\sum_{k\in\{0,m\}}e^{\frac{{\rm i}k\pi}{m}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}C_{j,0}\lambda_{k}^{n-2m}\int_{0}^{1}e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{k}s|x|}(1-s)^{n-3-j}{\rm d}s\right),

(2.2)

and

\begin{split}R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})(x)=&\sum_{k\in I^{\pm}}|x|^{2m-n}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{2m-3}C_{l}\int_{0}^{1}e^{{\rm i}s\lambda_{k}|x|}(1-s)^{2m-l-3}{\rm d}s\right)\\
&+\sum_{k\in I^{\pm}}\sum_{l=2m-2}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}D_{l}\lambda_{k}^{l+2-2m}|x|^{l+2-n}e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{k}|x|},\end{split}

(2.3)

hold for some constants
C_{j,0},C_{l},D_{l}
.

2.2.
The perturbed resolvent expansions around zero energy

In order to study the spectral measure of
H
near zero energy, we set
R^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m}):=(H-\lambda^{2m}\mp{\rm i}0)^{-1}
, which is well defined under our assumptions on
V
(see
[
1
]
). Denote

M^{\pm}(\lambda)=U+vR_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})v,\quad v(x)=|V(x)|^{\frac{1}{2}},\,\,U=\sgn V,

where
\sgn x=1
when
x\geq 0
and
\sgn x=-1
when
x<0
. If
(M^{\pm}(\lambda))^{-1}
exist in
L^{2}
, one checks the following symmetric resolvent identities

R_{V}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})=R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})-R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})v(M^{\pm}(\lambda))^{-1}vR_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m}).

(2.4)

Set

T_{0}=U+b_{0}vG_{2m-n}v,\quad b_{0}=(4\pi)^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{2m-2}\frac{c_{j}}{(2m-2-j)!},

where
c_{j}
is defined in (
2.1
) and

(G_{2m-n}f)(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|x-y|^{2m-n}f(y)\,{\rm d}y.

Denoted by
S_{m-\frac{n}{2}}
the orthogonal projection from
L^{2}
to
\mbox{ker}\,T_{0}
, the following expansions have already been proved in
[
11
]
.

Theorem 2.1
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.1
, there exists some
\lambda_{0}\in(0,1)
such that
M^{\pm}(\lambda)
have bounded inverses in
L^{2}
for all
0<\lambda<\lambda_{0}
, and the following statements hold.

(i) If
0
is not an eigenvalue of
H
, we have

(M^{\pm}(\lambda))^{-1}=D_{0}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{[\frac{n}{2m}]-1}\lambda^{2mj}B_{j}+\Gamma_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda),\quad 0<\lambda<\lambda_{0},

where
D_{0}
,
B_{j}
and
\Gamma_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda)
are bounded operators in
L^{2}
, and

\Gamma_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda)\in\mathfrak{S}^{n-2m}_{\frac{n+1}{2}}\big((0,\lambda_{0})\big).

(ii) If
0
is an eigenvalue of
H
, we have

(M^{\pm}(\lambda))^{-1}=\lambda^{-2m}S_{m-\frac{n}{2}}D_{1}S_{m-\frac{n}{2}}+Q_{m-\frac{n}{2}}D_{0}Q_{m-\frac{n}{2}}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{[\frac{n}{2m}]-1}\lambda^{2m(j-1)}B_{j}+\Gamma_{1}^{\pm}(\lambda),\quad 0<\lambda<\lambda_{0},

where
D_{1}
,
B_{j}
and
\Gamma_{1}^{\pm}(\lambda)
are bounded operators in
L^{2}
,

\Gamma_{2m-\frac{n}{2},2m-\frac{n}{2}}^{\pm}(\lambda)\in\mathfrak{S}^{n-6m}_{\frac{n+1}{2}}\big((0,\lambda_{0})\big),

and
Q_{m-\frac{n}{2}}=I-S_{m-\frac{n}{2}}
.

2.3.
Integrals with point and line singularities

We now introduce some estimates for integrals with point and at most two different types of line singularities, which will be frequently used especially in the high energy part estimate. We note that these results are valid for all dimensions
n
but not only the odd ones.

The following lemma can be seen in
[
15
, Lemma 3.8]
or
[
5
, Lemma 6.3]
.

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
n\geq 1
. Then there is some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|x-y|^{-k}\langle y\rangle^{-l}\,{\rm d}y\leq C\langle x\rangle^{-\min\{k,\,k+l-n\}},

provided
l\geq 0
,
0\leq k<n
and
k+l>n
.

The following lemma was already introduced in
[
2
]
, whose proof is almost the same to that of
[
5
, Lemma 6.3]
.

Lemma 2.3
.

Suppose
n\geq 1
,
k_{1},l_{1}\in[0,n)
,
k_{2},l_{2}\in[0,+\infty)
,
\beta\in(0,+\infty)
, and
k_{2}+l_{2}+\beta\geq n
. It follows uniformly in
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
that

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle|x-y|^{-k_{1}}\rangle\langle|y-z|^{-l_{1}}\rangle\langle y-y_{0}\rangle^{-\beta-}}{\langle x-y\rangle^{k_{2}}\langle y-z\rangle^{l_{2}}}{\rm d}y\\
\lesssim&\begin{cases}\langle|x-z|^{-\max\{0,k_{1}+l_{1}-n\}}\rangle\langle x-z\rangle^{-\min\{k_{2},l_{2},k_{2}+l_{2}+\beta-n\}},\quad&k_{1}+l_{1}\neq n,\\
\langle|x-z|^{0-}\rangle\langle x-z\rangle^{-\min\{k_{2},l_{2},k_{2}+l_{2}+\beta-n\}},&k_{1}+l_{1}=n.\end{cases}\end{split}

We now turn to integrals with both point singularities line singularities when
n\geq 2
. Given separated
w,w^{\prime},x,z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we define quantities

E_{xyyz}=\mbox{$\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}-\frac{y-z}{|y-z|}$},\quad E_{ww^{\prime}xy}=\mbox{$\frac{w-w^{\prime}}{|w-w^{\prime}|}-\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}$}.

(2.5)

Proposition 2.4
.

Suppose
n\geq 2
,
k_{1},l_{1}\in[0,n)
,
k_{2},l_{2}\in[0,+\infty)
,
\beta\in(0,+\infty)
,
k_{2}+l_{2}+\beta\geq n
, and
p,q\in[0,n-1)
. It follows uniformly in
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
that

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle|x-y|^{-k_{1}}\rangle\langle|y-z|^{-l_{1}}\rangle\langle y-y_{0}\rangle^{-\beta-}}{\langle x-y\rangle^{k_{2}}\langle y-z\rangle^{l_{2}}|E_{xyyz}|^{p}|E_{ww^{\prime}xy}|^{q}}{\rm d}y\\
\lesssim&|E_{ww^{\prime}xz}|^{-q}\begin{cases}\langle|x-z|^{-\max\{0,k_{1}+l_{1}-n\}}\rangle\langle x-z\rangle^{-\min\{k_{2},l_{2},k_{2}+l_{2}+\beta-n,k_{2}+l_{2}-\max\{p,q\}\}},\,&k_{1}+l_{1}\neq n,\\
\langle|x-z|^{0-}\rangle\langle x-z\rangle^{-\min\{k_{2},l_{2},k_{2}+l_{2}+\beta-n,k_{2}+l_{2}-\max\{p,q\}\}},&k_{1}+l_{1}=n.\end{cases}\end{split}

The tedious proof of Proposition
2.4
will be given in Appendix
A
, which is based on first proving the special case
q=0
that will also be frequently used later in Section
5.4
.

2.4.
Two oscillatory integral estimates

Given
\lambda_{0}>0
, we will need estimates of one dimensional oscillatory integral in the form of

I(t,x)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}+{\rm i}x\lambda}\psi(\lambda){\rm d}\lambda,\quad t\neq 0,\,x\in\mathbb{R}.

where
\psi
is supported in
[0,\lambda_{0}]
or
[\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2},+\infty)
. Denoted by

\mbox{$\mu_{b}=\frac{m-1-b}{2m-1}$},

(2.6)

the following two estimates on one dimensional oscillatory integrals can be found in
[
17
]
.

Lemma 2.5
.

Suppose
N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
b\in\mathbb{R}
and
\psi\in S_{N}^{b}((0,\lambda_{0}))
supported in
[0,\lambda_{0}]
.

(i) If
b\in[-\frac{1}{2},\,2Nm-1)
, then

|I(t,x)|\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{1+b}{2m}}(|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x|)^{-\mu_{b}},\quad|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x|\gtrsim 1.

(2.7)

(ii) If
b\in(-1,\,2Nm-1)
, then

|I(t,x)|\lesssim(1+|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}})^{-(1+b)},\quad 0<|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x|\lesssim 1.

(2.8)

Lemma 2.6
.

Suppose
N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
b\in\mathbb{R}
and
\psi\in S_{N}^{b}((\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2},+\infty))
supported in
[\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2},+\infty)
.

(i) If
b\in[m-1-N(2m-1),2Nm-1)
, then

|I(t,x)|\lesssim\begin{cases}|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{b}}|x|^{-\mu_{b}},\quad&|t|\gtrsim 1,\,|t|^{-1}|x|\gtrsim 1,\\
|t|^{-N},&|t|\gtrsim 1,\,|t|^{-1}|x|<<1.\end{cases}

(2.9)

(ii) If
b\in[-\frac{1}{2},2Nm-1)
, then

|I(t,x)|\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{1+b}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x|\right)^{-\mu_{b}},\quad 0<|t|\lesssim 1,\,x\in\mathbb{R}.

(2.10)

The constants in estimates (
2.7
), (
2.8
), (
2.9
) and (
2.10
) stay bounded when the seminorms of
\psi
in
S_{N}^{b}
stay bounded.

3.
The proof of Theorem
1.1
(low energy part)

By an iteration of the resolvent identity and (
2.4
), we have

\displaystyle e^{-{\rm i}tH}\chi(H)P_{ac}(H)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{2N}\frac{(-1)^{k}m}{\pi{\rm i}}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}}\left(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(VR_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m}))^{k}-R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(VR_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m}))^{k}\right)\lambda^{2m-1}\chi(\lambda^{2m}){\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{\pi{\rm i}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}}(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{+}vM^{+}(\lambda)^{-1}vR_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(VR_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m}))^{N}\lambda^{2m-1}\chi(\lambda^{2m}){\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle+\frac{m}{\pi{\rm i}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}}(R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{-}vM^{-}(\lambda)^{-1}vR_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(VR_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m}))^{N}\lambda^{2m-1}\chi(\lambda^{2m}){\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{2N}\Omega^{low}_{k}-\left(\Omega^{+,low}_{r}-\Omega^{-,low}_{r}\right),

(3.1)

where
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
is fixed and will be chosen later.
Since

\Omega^{low}_{0}=e^{-{\rm i}tH_{0}}\chi(H_{0})
,
by
[
19
,
17
]
, we have

\left|\Omega^{low}_{0}(t,x,y)\right|\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad t\neq 0,\,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.2)

We next estimate the kernels of the initial terms
\Omega^{low}_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq 2N
, and then prove estimate for the kernel of the remainder term
\Omega^{+,low}_{r}-\Omega^{-,low}_{r}
. Theorem
1.1
(low energy part) shall follow immediately from (
3.2
), and (
3.3
), (
3.6
) below.

3.1.
Estimate for
\Omega^{low}_{k}(t,x,y)

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we shall prove in this part that if
|V(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-(n+2)-}
, then the kernel of
\Omega^{low}_{k}
satisfies

\left|\Omega^{low}_{k}(t,x,y)\right|\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}}.

(3.3)

Note that the kernel
\Omega^{low}_{k}(t,x,y)
can be expressed by

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{k}m}{\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}}\left(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(z_{0}-z_{1})\prod_{j=1}^{k}(V(z_{j})R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(z_{j}-z_{j+1}))\right.

\displaystyle\left.-R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(z_{0}-z_{1})\prod_{j=1}^{k}(V(z_{j})R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(z_{j}-z_{j+1}))\right)\lambda^{2m-1}\chi(\lambda^{2m}){\rm d}z_{1}\cdots{\rm d}z_{k}{\rm d}\lambda,

where
z_{0}=x,\ z_{k+1}=y
, we further decompose this integral by restricting the integration in
\mathbb{R}^{nk}
to

U_{1}=\left\{(z_{1},\ldots,z_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{nk}:\,|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}(|z_{0}-z_{1}|+\cdots+|z_{k}-z_{k+1}|)\leq 1\right\},\,\quad U_{2}=\mathbb{R}^{nk}\setminus U_{2},

and denote the part in
U_{i}
by
\Omega^{low,i}_{k}(t,x,y)
.

By (
2.2
), (
2.3
) and the algebraic identity

\prod_{j=0}^{k+1}A_{j}^{+}-\prod_{j=0}^{k+1}A_{j}^{-}=\sum_{j=0}^{k+1}A_{0}^{-}\cdots(A_{j}^{+}-A_{j}^{-})A_{j+1}^{+}\cdots A_{k}^{+},

\Omega^{low,1}_{k}(t,x,y)
can be written as a linear combination of

\begin{split}\int_{[0,1]^{k+1}}&\int_{U_{1}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}+{\rm i}\lambda_{k_{j_{0}}}s_{j_{0}}|z_{j_{0}-1}-z_{j_{0}}|+\sum_{j=1,j\neq j_{0}}^{k+1}{\rm i}\lambda_{k_{j}}s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|}\prod_{j=1,j\neq j_{0}}^{k+1}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|^{-t_{j}}\\
&\times\prod_{j=1}^{k}V(z_{j})\lambda^{n-1+\sigma}\chi(\lambda^{2m})\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}(1-s_{j})^{q_{j}}{\rm d}\lambda{\rm d}z_{1}\cdots{\rm d}z_{k}{\rm d}s_{1}\cdots{\rm d}s_{k+1},\end{split}

where
\sigma\geqslant 0
,
\lambda_{k_{j}}=\lambda\exp{(\frac{{\rm i}k_{j}z}{m})}
,
\lambda_{k_{j_{0}}}=\lambda\,\mbox{or}\,-\lambda
,
1\leq j_{0}\leq k+1
,

\begin{cases}k_{j}\in\{1,\cdots,m\}\qquad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{ if }\ j<j_{0},\\
k_{j}\in\{0,\cdots,m-1\}\ \,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{ if }\ j_{0}<j\leq k+1,\\
t_{j}\in\{\frac{n-1}{2},\cdots,n-2m\}\ \,\,\,\,\,\mbox{ for }\ j=1,\cdots,k+1,\ j\neq j_{0},\\
p_{j}\in\{0,1\}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\qquad\qquad\mbox{ for }\ j\neq j_{0},\end{cases}

q_{j}\in\{0,\cdots,2m-3\}
, and
q_{j_{0}}\in\{\frac{n-3}{2},\frac{n-1}{2},\cdots,n-3\}
.
Since in
U_{1}
, it follows that

|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}\left|\sum_{j=1,j\neq j_{0}}^{k+1}(s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|)\pm s_{j_{0}}|z_{j_{0}-1}-z_{j_{0}}|\right|\leq 1,

and
|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\leq 1
by the triangle inequality, we can apply Lemma
2.5
with
b=n-1
to have

\displaystyle|\Omega^{low,1}_{k}(t,x,y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}\prod_{j=1,j\neq j_{0}}^{k+1}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|^{-t_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\langle z_{j}\rangle^{-\beta}dz_{1}\cdots dz_{k}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
2.2
.

\Omega^{low,2}_{k}(t,x,y)
is, apart from a constant, the integral

\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{U_{2}}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}}R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})(z_{0}-z_{1})\prod_{j=1}^{k}\left(V(z_{j})R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})(z_{j}-z_{j+1})\right)\lambda^{2m-1}\chi(\lambda^{2m}){\rm d}\lambda{\rm d}z_{1}\cdots{\rm d}z_{k},

and we will only consider the sign
"+"
here since the other case is similar. We further split
U_{2}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}U_{2,i}
where

U_{2,i}=\left\{(z_{1},\ldots,z_{k})\in U_{2}:\,|z_{i-1}-z_{i}|\geq|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|\,\mbox{for all}\,j=1,\cdots,k+1\right\}.

Without loss of generality, we only consider the integral in
U_{2,1}
which, by (
2.3
), can be written as a linear combination of

\begin{split}&\int_{[0,1]^{k}}\int_{U_{2,1}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}+{\rm i}\lambda_{k_{1}}|z_{0}-z_{1}|+\sum_{j=2}^{k+1}{\rm i}\lambda_{k_{j}}s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|}\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|^{-t_{j}}\\
&\times\prod_{j=1}^{k}V(z_{j})\prod_{j=2}^{k+1}(1-s_{j})^{q_{j}}\lambda^{n-1-t_{1}+\sigma}\chi(\lambda^{2m}){\rm d}\lambda{\rm d}z_{1}\cdots{\rm d}z_{k}{\rm d}s_{2}\cdots{\rm d}s_{k+1},\end{split}

(3.4)

where
\sigma\geq 0
,
\lambda_{k_{j}}=\lambda\exp(\frac{{\rm i}k_{j}\pi}{m})
and
k_{j}\in\{0,\cdots,m-1\}
for
j=1,\cdots,k+1
,
p_{j}\in\{0,1\}
,
q_{j}\in\{0,\cdots,2m-3\}
and

\begin{cases}t_{1}\in\{\frac{n-1}{2},\cdots,n-2\},\\
t_{j}\in\{\frac{n-1}{2},\frac{n+1}{2},\cdots,n-2m\}\,\,\,\mbox{ for}\,\,j\neq 1.\end{cases}

In order to apply Lemma
2.5
to estimate (
3.4
), we write

\begin{split}e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{k_{1}}|z_{0}-z_{1}|+\sum_{j=2}^{k+1}{\rm i}\lambda_{k_{j}}s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|}=&e^{{\rm i}\lambda|z_{0}-z_{1}|+\sum_{j=2}^{k+1}{\rm i}\lambda s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|}\\
&\times e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{k}|z_{0}-z_{1}|-{\rm i}\lambda|z_{0}-z_{1}|+\sum_{j=2}^{k+1}({\rm i}\lambda_{k_{j}}s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|-{\rm i}\lambda s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|)}.\end{split}

(3.5)

One easily checks with respect to
\lambda
that

e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{k}|z_{0}-z_{1}|-{\rm i}\lambda|z_{0}-z_{1}|+\sum_{j=2}^{k+1}({\rm i}\lambda_{k_{j}}s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|-{\rm i}\lambda s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|)}\in S^{0}((0,1)),\quad k=0,\cdots,m-1,

where the seminorms are uniformly bounded in parameters, so we can plug the identity (
3.5
) into (
3.4
), and apply Lemma
2.5
with
b=n-1-t_{1}
to obtain

\begin{split}|\eqref{eq4.60}|\lesssim&\int_{U_{2,1}}\int_{[0,1]^{k}}|t|^{-\frac{n-t_{1}}{2m}}\left(|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}\left(|z_{0}-z_{1}|+\sum_{j=2}^{k+1}s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|\right)\right)^{-\frac{m-1-(n-1-t_{1})}{2m-1}}\\
&\times\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}|z_{0}-z_{1}|^{-t_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\langle z_{j}\rangle^{-\beta}{\rm d}s_{2}\cdots{\rm d}s_{k+1}{\rm d}z_{1}\cdots{\rm d}z_{k}\\
\lesssim&\int_{U_{2,1}}|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|z_{0}-z_{1}|)^{-t_{1}-\frac{m-1-(n-1-t_{1})}{2m-1}}\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}|z_{0}-z_{1}|^{-t_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\langle z_{j}\rangle^{-\beta}{\rm d}z_{1}\cdots{\rm d}z_{k}\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\end{split}

where in the second inequality, we have used Lemma
2.2
and the fact in
U_{2,1}
that

|z_{0}-z_{1}|\sim|z_{0}-z_{1}|+\sum_{j=z}^{k+1}s_{j}^{p_{j}}|z_{j-1}-z_{j+1}|,\quad|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|z_{0}-z_{1}|\gtrsim 1;

in the last inequality, we have used the facts that

|z_{0}-z_{1}|\gtrsim\sum_{j=1}^{k+1}{|z_{j-1}-z_{j}|}\geq|x-y|
holds in
U_{2,1}
, and that
t_{1}+\frac{m-1-(n-1-t_{1})}{2m-1}\geq\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}
holds when
t_{1}\geq\frac{n-1}{2}
. Therefore we complete the proof of (
3.3
).

3.2.
Estimate for the remainder term
(\Omega_{r}^{+,low}-\Omega_{r}^{-,low})(t,x,y)

Let
N>[\frac{n}{2m}]+2
in (
3.1
). The aim of this part is to prove that the kernel of
\Omega_{r}^{+,low}-\Omega_{r}^{-,low}
satisfies

\left|(\Omega_{r}^{+,low}-\Omega_{r}^{-,low})(t,x,y)\right|\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-(\frac{n}{2m}-\sigma)}(1+|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}})\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},

(3.6)

where
\sigma=0
if zero is not an eigenvalue of
H
, and
\sigma=2
otherwise.

We first introduce the following two lemmas, whose proofs are given in Appendix
B
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
n>4m
be odd,
l\in\{[\frac{n}{2m}]+3,\cdots\}
,
s_{i}\geq 0
be integers with
s_{0}+\cdots+s_{l}\leq\frac{n+1}{2}
, and
R_{0}^{\pm,(s_{i})}(\lambda^{2m})
be the operators with integral kernels
\partial_{\lambda}^{s_{i}}\big[R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})(x-y)\big]
.
If
|V(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-(n+2)-}
, then for each
k\in I^{\pm}
and
\tau\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
, it follows that

\left\|v(y)\left(\prod_{j=0}^{l-1}(R_{0}^{\pm,(s_{j})}(\lambda^{2m})V)\partial_{\lambda}^{s_{l}}\left(|x-\cdot|^{-\tau}e^{\mp{\rm i}\lambda s|x|}e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{k}s|x-y|}\right)\right)(y)\right\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\lesssim\lambda^{-s_{l}}\langle x\rangle^{-\tau},

(3.7)

where the estimate holds uniformly in
\lambda,s\in(0,1)
, and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Further,
we also have for each
k\in I^{+}
,
j\in\{0,\cdots,l-1\}
and
\tau\in\{\frac{n-1}{2},\cdots,n-2m\}
that

\displaystyle\big\|v(y)\left(R_{0}^{-,(s_{0})}(\lambda^{2m})\right.V

\displaystyle\cdots\left(R_{0}^{+,(s_{j})}(\lambda^{2m})-R_{0}^{-,(s_{j})}(\lambda^{2m})\right)V\cdots R_{0}^{+,(s_{l-2})}(\lambda^{2m})

(3.8)

\displaystyle\left.\times VR_{0}^{+,(s_{l-1})}(\lambda^{2m})V\partial_{\lambda}^{s_{l}}\left(|x-\cdot|^{-\tau}e^{-{\rm i}\lambda s|x|}e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{k}s|x-y|}\right)\right)(y)\big\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\lesssim{\lambda^{n-2m-s_{j}}},

which holds uniformly in
\lambda\in(0,1)
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume that
n>4m
is odd,
|V(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-(n+2)-}
and
l>[\frac{n}{2m}]+2
, then

v(R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{l}R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})(x-\cdot)=\int_{0}^{1}e^{\pm{\rm i}\lambda|x|}\omega_{1,0}^{\pm}(\lambda,s,\cdot,x){\rm d}s+\int_{0}^{1}e^{\pm{\rm i}\lambda s|x|}\omega_{1,1}^{\pm}(\lambda,s,\cdot,x){\rm d}s,

(3.9)

where

\langle x\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\omega_{1,q}^{\pm}(\lambda,s,\cdot,x)\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{0}((0,1),\,\,\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}}),\quad q=0,1.

(3.10)

We also have

\begin{split}&v\left((R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{l}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(x-\cdot)-(R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{l}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(x-\cdot)\right)\\
=&\sum_{i=0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{1}e^{{\rm i}\lambda s^{q}|x|}\omega_{2,i}^{+}(\lambda,s,\cdot,x){\rm d}s-\int_{0}^{1}e^{-{\rm i}\lambda s^{q}|x|}\omega_{2,i}^{-}(\lambda,s,\cdot,x){\rm d}s\right),\end{split}

(3.11)

and for
i=0,1
that

\omega_{2,i}^{\pm}(\lambda,s,\cdot,x)\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-2m}((0,1),\,\,\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}}),

(3.12)

\langle x\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\omega_{2,i}^{\pm}(\lambda,s,\cdot,x)\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}((0,1),\,\,\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}}).

(3.13)

Now we are ready to prove the results for
\Omega_{r}^{+,low}-\Omega_{r}^{-,low}
in (
3.1
). If
\lambda_{0}
is given by Theorem
2.1
, note that
\mathrm{supp}\,\chi(\lambda^{2m})\subset[-\lambda_{0},\lambda_{0}]
, then it suffices to estimate

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}

\displaystyle e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}}\chi(\lambda^{2m})\left(\left\langle(M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}v(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-y),v(R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-x)\right\rangle\right.

(3.14)

\displaystyle-\left.\left\langle(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1}v(R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-y),v(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-x)\right\rangle\right)\lambda^{2m-1}{\rm d}\lambda.

If we set

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{1}(\lambda,x,y)=

\displaystyle\left\langle(M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}v\left((R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-y)-(R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-y)\right),\right.

\displaystyle\left.v(R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-x)\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{2}(\lambda,x,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\left((M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}-(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1}\right)v(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-y),\,v(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-x)\right\rangle,

and

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{3}(\lambda,x,y)=

\displaystyle\left\langle(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1}v(R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-y),\right.

\displaystyle\left.\,v\left((R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-x)-(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})V)^{N}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(\cdot-x)\right)\right\rangle,

then we can rewrite (
3.14
) as

\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-{\rm i}t\lambda^{2m}}\chi(\lambda^{2m})(\Upsilon_{1}(\lambda,x,y)+\Upsilon_{2}(\lambda,x,y)+\Upsilon_{3}(\lambda,x,y))\lambda^{2m-1}{\rm d}\lambda.

It follows from (
3.9
)–(
3.11
) that
\Upsilon_{1}(\lambda,x,y)
has the following expression

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\sum_{p,q=0,1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}e^{\pm{\rm i}\lambda s_{1}^{p}|y|+{\rm i}\lambda s_{2}^{q}|x|}\left\langle(M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{2,p}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{-}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle{\rm d}s_{1}{\rm d}s_{2},

\Upsilon_{2}(\lambda,x,y)
can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=0,1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}e^{{\rm i}\lambda s_{1}^{p}|y|-{\rm i}\lambda s_{2}^{q}|x|}\left\langle((M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}-(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1})\omega_{1,p}^{+}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{+}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle{\rm d}s_{1}{\rm d}s_{2},

and
\Upsilon_{3}(\lambda,x,y)
can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\sum_{p,q=0,1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}e^{-{\rm i}\lambda s_{1}^{p}|y|\mp{\rm i}\lambda s_{2}^{q}|x|}\left\langle(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{1,p}^{-}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{2,q}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle{\rm d}s_{1}{\rm d}s_{2}.

Case 1:

|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}(|x|+|y|)\leq 1
.

If the zero energy of
H
is regular, then Theorem
2.1
, (
3.10
) and (
3.12
) yield

\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle(M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{2,p}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{-}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-1}((0,\lambda_{0})),

\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{1,p}^{-}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{2,q}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-1}((0,\lambda_{0})),

\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle((M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}-(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1})\omega_{1,p}^{+}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{+}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-1}((0,\lambda_{0})).

Thus, we use (
2.8
) in Lemma
2.5
with
b=n-1
to obtain

\left|\eqref{eq4.64}\right|\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}}.

(3.15)

On the other hand, if
0
is an eigenvalue of
H
, then Theorem
2.1
, (
3.11
) and (
3.12
) imply

\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle(M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{2,p}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{-}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-2m-1}((0,\lambda_{0})),

\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{1,p}^{-}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{2,q}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-2m-1}((0,\lambda_{0})),

\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle((M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}-(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1})\omega_{1,p}^{+}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{+}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-4m-1}((0,\lambda_{0})).

Thus, we use (
2.8
) in Lemma
2.5
with
b=n-4m-1
to obtain

\left|\eqref{eq4.64}\right|\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-\frac{n-4m}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}}.

(3.16)

Case 2:

|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}(|x|+|y|)\geq 1
.

If the zero energy of
H
is regular, then Theorem
2.1
, (
3.10
) and (
3.13
) yield

\langle x\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle(M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{2,p}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{-}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}((0,\lambda_{0})),

\langle x\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{1,p}^{-}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{2,q}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}((0,\lambda_{0})),

\langle x\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle((M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}-(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1})\omega_{1,p}^{+}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{+}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-1}((0,\lambda_{0})).

Thus we apply Lemma
2.5
with
b=\frac{n-1}{2}
to get

\displaystyle\left|\eqref{eq4.64}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-\frac{\frac{n+1}{2}}{2m}}\left(|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}(|x|+|y|)\right)^{-\frac{m-\frac{n+1}{2}}{2m-1}}\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}

(3.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},

where in the last inequality, we have used the identity

\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}+\frac{m-\frac{n+1}{2}}{2m-1}=\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}$}.

On the other hand, if
0
is an eigenvalue of
H
, then Theorem
2.1
, (
3.10
) and (
3.13
) yield

\small\begin{split}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle(M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{2,p}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{-}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}-2m}((0,\lambda_{0})),\\
\langle x\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1}\omega_{1,p}^{-}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{2,q}^{\pm}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{\frac{n-1}{2}-2m}((0,\lambda_{0})),\\
\langle x\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\lambda^{2m-1}\left\langle((M^{+}(\lambda))^{-1}-(M^{-}(\lambda))^{-1})\omega_{1,p}^{+}(\lambda,s_{1},\cdot,y),\,\omega_{1,q}^{+}(\lambda,s_{2},\cdot,x)\right\rangle\in S_{\frac{n+1}{2}}^{n-4m-1}((0,\lambda_{0})).\end{split}

Since
n-4m-1\geq\frac{n-1}{2}-2m
when
n>4m
, we can apply Lemma
2.5
with
b=\frac{n-1}{2}-2m
to get

\displaystyle\left|\eqref{eq4.64}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-\frac{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}{2m}}\left(|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}(|x|+|y|)\right)^{-\min\left\{\frac{3m-\frac{n+1}{2}}{2m-1},0\right\}}\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}

(3.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n-2m}{2m}}\left(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|\right)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}}.

Here, we have used
\langle x\rangle^{-1}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\leq(|x|+|y|)^{-1}\leq|x-y|^{-1}
and

\mbox{$\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}\leq\min\{\frac{3m-\frac{n+1}{2}}{2m-1},0\}+\frac{n-1}{2}$}.

Now it follows from (
3.15
)-(
3.18
) that the kernel of
\Omega_{r}^{+,low}-\Omega_{r}^{-,low}
satisfies that
(
3.6
).

4.
The proof of Theorem
1.1
(high energy part)

4.1.
Overview

Given
K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we apply the resolvent identity

R^{\pm}(\lambda)=\sum_{k=0}^{2K-1}(-1)^{k}R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda)(VR_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda))^{k}+(R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda)V)^{K}R^{\pm}(\lambda)(VR_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda))^{K},

to the Stone’s formula of
e^{-\mathrm{i}tH}P_{ac}(H)\tilde{\chi}(H)
, then

e^{-\mathrm{i}tH}P_{ac}(H)\tilde{\chi}(H)=\sum_{k=0}^{2K-1}\Omega_{k}^{high}+\Omega_{K,r}^{+,high}-\Omega_{K,r}^{-,high},

where

\Omega_{k}^{high}=\frac{(-1)^{k}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda)\left(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda)(VR_{0}^{+}(\lambda))^{k}-R_{0}^{-}(\lambda)(VR_{0}^{-}(\lambda))^{k}\right){\rm d}\lambda,

\Omega_{K,r}^{\pm,high}=\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda)(R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda)V)^{K}R^{\pm}(\lambda)(VR_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda))^{K}{\rm d}\lambda.

(4.1)

These integrals converge in weak* sense when
V
the assumption of Theorem
1.1
, and
K
will be chosen sufficiently large later.

The distribution kernel of
\Omega_{0}^{high}
is

\Omega_{0}^{high}(t,x,y)=\mathscr{F}^{-1}\left(\tilde{\chi}(|\cdot|^{2m})e^{-{\rm i}t|\cdot|^{2m}}\right)(x-y),

by the Fourier representation of the spectral measure of
(-\Delta)^{m}
, and the estimate for
\Omega_{0}^{high}(t,x,y)
has already been shown in
[
17
, Lemma 2.1]
.

The distribution kernel of
\Omega_{k}^{high}
when
k\geq 1
is formally the repeated integral

\begin{split}&\Omega_{k}^{high}(t,x,y)\\
=&\frac{(-1)^{k}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\left(\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i=0}^{k}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda)(r_{i})-\prod\limits_{i=0}^{k}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda)(r_{i})$}\right)\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i=1}^{k}V(x_{i})$}dx_{1}\cdots dx_{k}\right){\rm d}\lambda,\end{split}

where
r_{i}=x_{i}-x_{i+1}
,
x_{0}=x
and
x_{k+1}=y
. For every fix
k\in\{1,\cdots,2K-1\}
, let

X=|r_{0}|+\cdots+|r_{k}|,\quad T=|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}}+|t|,\quad\mathbb{K}=\{1,\cdots,k\},\quad\mathbb{K}_{0}=\{0,\cdots,k\}.

We fix a sufficiently small
\delta>0
which will be chosen later, take
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
where
\operatorname{supp}\phi\subset[-1,1]
and
\phi=1
in
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]
, and further decompose
\Omega_{k}^{high}(t,x,y)
into

\begin{split}&\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)=\frac{(-1)^{k}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda)\\
&\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\left(\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda)(r_{i})-\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda)(r_{i})$}\right)\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V(x_{i})$}\phi(\mbox{$\frac{X}{\delta T}$})dx_{1}\cdots dx_{k}\right){\rm d}\lambda,\end{split}

(4.2)

and

\begin{split}&\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)=\frac{(-1)^{k}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda)\\
&\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\left(\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda)(r_{i})-\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda)(r_{i})$}\right)\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V(x_{i})$}(1-\phi(\mbox{$\frac{X}{\delta T}$}))dx_{1}\cdots dx_{k}\right){\rm d}\lambda.\end{split}

(4.3)

To prove Theorem
1.1
(high energy part), we summarize what remains to show:

(i)

Show the existence of a sufficiently large
K
such that

|\Omega_{K,r}^{\pm,high}(t,x,y)|\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad|t|>0,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

The proof will not be presented in this paper, because it is exactly the same one in
[
2
]
, and
K>[\frac{n-4}{4}]+2
suffices to give such estimate.

(ii)

For any fixed
k\geq 1
, show the existence of a sufficiently small
\delta>0
depending on
\lambda_{0}
such that

|\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)|\begin{cases}\lesssim_{N}|t|^{-N},\quad|t|\gtrsim 1,\,0<|x-y|\lesssim\delta|t|,\,N\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}},\quad 0<|t|\lesssim 1,\,0<|x-y|\lesssim\delta|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}}.\end{cases}

(4.4)

The proof is a slight adjustment of that in the low dimensional case
[
2
]
, and we will present the proof later in Section
4.2
for the sake of completeness.

(iii)

For any fixed
\delta>0
and
k\geq 1
, show

|\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)|\lesssim\begin{cases}|t|^{-\frac{n}{2}}(1+|t|^{-1}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad&|t|\gtrsim 1,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad&0<|t|\lesssim 1,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.\end{cases}

(4.5)

This estimate is the subtlest one in the paper where the regularity of
V
is involved, and we will first sketch the modules of its proof in Section
4.3
, leaving the lengthy technical details for a particularly complicated part in the next Section
5
.

We also remark that the RHS of (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) are stronger than claimed in Theorem
1.1
(high energy part).

4.2.
Estimate for
\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)

To show (
4.4
), we only need to assume
V\in L^{\infty}
. Since
\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)=0
when
|x-y|>\delta T
, we only need to consider its estimates when
0<|x-y|\leq\delta T
. We start by scaling the formal expression (
4.2
) of
\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)
:

\begin{split}\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)=&\frac{(-1)^{k}m}{\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1}\\
&\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\left(\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})-\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})$}\right)\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V(x_{i})$}\phi(\mbox{$\frac{X}{\delta T}$})dx_{1}\cdots dx_{k}\right){\rm d}\lambda.\end{split}

Note that

\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})-\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})=\sum\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}\left(R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})-R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})\right)\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}R_{0}^{\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i^{\prime}}),

where

\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}-,\quad&i^{\prime}<i,\\
+,&i^{\prime}>i,\end{cases}

and we know by (
2.2
) that
R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})-R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})
is a finite linear combination of the form

\lambda^{n-2m}\int_{0}^{1}e^{\pm{\rm i}s\lambda|r_{i}|}(1-s)^{n-3-j}{\rm d}s,\quad\mbox{$j=0,\cdots,\frac{n-3}{2}$}.

Also note that when
\lambda>0
,
R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i^{\prime}})
is a finite linear combination of the form

e^{\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda|r_{i^{\prime}}|}|r_{i^{\prime}}|^{-(n-2-l)}\lambda^{-(2m-2-l)}f_{l}(\lambda,r_{i^{\prime}}),\quad\mbox{$l=0,\cdots,\frac{n-3}{2}$},

where

|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}f_{l}(\lambda,r_{i^{\prime}})|\lesssim_{j}\lambda^{-j},\quad\lambda>0,\,r_{i^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\},\,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

We know from the above facts that
\Omega_{k}^{high,1}(t,x,y)
is the linear combination of

\begin{split}&\Omega_{k,i,j,\vec{l},\vec{\delta}}^{high,1}(t,x,y)\\
:=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\phi(\mbox{$\frac{X}{\delta T}$})V(x_{1})\cdots V(x_{k})\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}|r_{i^{\prime}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}\\
&\times\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}+\mathrm{i}\lambda(\delta_{0}|r_{0}|\cdots+\delta_{k}|r_{k}|)}g_{\vec{l},i}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m}){\rm d}\lambda\right)(1-s)^{n-3-j}{\rm d}s{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\\
&\mbox{$i=0,\cdots,k,\quad j=0,\cdots,\frac{n-3}{2},$}\\
&\mbox{$\vec{l}=\{l_{0},\cdots,l_{k}\}\setminus\{l_{i}\}\in\left\{0,\cdots,\frac{n-3}{2}\right\}^{k},\quad\vec{\delta}=\{\delta_{0},\cdots,\delta_{k}\}\in\{-1,1\}^{k+1}.$}\end{split}

(4.6)

where

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{q}g_{\vec{l},i}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\right|\lesssim_{q}\lambda^{n-1-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})},\quad\lambda>0,\,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\},\,q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

To deal with the integral in
\lambda
, note that

g_{\vec{l},i}(\cdot,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\tilde{\chi}(\cdot^{2m})\in S^{n-1-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}\left((\mbox{$\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}$},+\infty)\right),

with seminorms uniformly bounded in
r_{0},\cdots,r_{k}
. In particular, if
\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})\geq 0
, we further have

g_{\vec{l},i}(\cdot,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\tilde{\chi}(\cdot^{2m})\in S^{n-1}\left((\mbox{$\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}$},+\infty)\right).

Also note that when
X\leq\delta T
we have
\left|\delta_{0}|r_{0}|\cdots+\delta_{k}|r_{k}|\right|\leq\delta T
, so if
\delta>0
is chosen sufficiently small, we can apply Lemma
2.6
to obtain when
X\leq\delta T
that

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}+\mathrm{i}\lambda(\delta_{0}|r_{0}|\cdots+\delta_{k}|r_{k}|)}g_{\vec{l},i}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m}){\rm d}\lambda\right|\\
\lesssim&\begin{cases}C_{N}|t|^{-N},\quad&|t|\gtrsim 1,\,N\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}},&0<|t|\lesssim 1,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})\geq 0,\\
|t|^{-\frac{n-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}{2m}},&0<|t|\lesssim 1,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})<0.\end{cases}\end{split}

Combining this and (
4.6
), we have the long time estimate:

\begin{split}|\Omega_{k,i,j,\vec{l},\vec{\delta}}^{high,1}(t,x,y)|&\lesssim_{N}|t|^{-N}\int_{\{X\lesssim\delta T\}}\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}|r_{i^{\prime}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}\\
&\lesssim_{N}|t|^{-N+kn-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(n-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}\\
&\lesssim_{N^{\prime}}|t|^{-N^{\prime}},\quad|t|\gtrsim 1,\,0<|x-y|\lesssim\delta|t|,\,N^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\end{split}

(4.7)

the short time estimate when
\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})\geq 0
:

\begin{split}|\Omega_{k,i,j,\vec{l},\vec{\delta}}^{high,1}(t,x,y)|&\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}\int_{\{X\lesssim\delta T\}}\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}|r_{i^{\prime}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}\\
&\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}+kn-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(n-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}\\
&=|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}+\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2+l_{i^{\prime}})}\\
&\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}},\quad 0<|t|\lesssim 1,\,0<|x-y|\lesssim\delta|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}},\end{split}

(4.8)

and the short time estimate when
\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})\leq 0
:

\begin{split}|\Omega_{k,i,j,\vec{l},\vec{\delta}}^{high,1}(t,x,y)|&\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}{2m}}\int_{\{X\lesssim\delta T\}}\prod\limits_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}|r_{i^{\prime}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}\\
&\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2m-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}{2m}+kn-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(n-2-l_{i^{\prime}})}\\
&=|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}+k+\frac{2m-1}{2m}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i\}}(2+l_{i^{\prime}})}\\
&\lesssim|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}},\quad 0<|t|\lesssim 1,\,0<|x-y|\lesssim\delta|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}},\end{split}

(4.9)

by scaling, and the estimates starting from the integral in
x_{i}
using Lemma
2.3
. Now the estimate (
4.4
) for
\Omega^{high,1}(t,x,y)
has been shown by (
4.7
) (
4.8
) and (
4.9
).

4.3.
The sketch of the estimate for
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)

To show (
4.5
), we need to assume
V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and (
1.4
). We start by the formal expression (
4.3
) of
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
which also has the form

\begin{split}&\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)=\frac{(-1)^{k}m}{\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}}\lambda^{2m-1}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\\
&\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\left(\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{+}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})-\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}R_{0}^{-}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})$}\right)\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V(x_{i})$}(1-\phi(\mbox{$\frac{X}{\delta T}$})){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}\right){\rm d}\lambda.\end{split}

When
\lambda>0
, we know from (
2.1
) that
R_{0}^{\pm}(\lambda^{2m})(r_{i})
is a finite linear combination of the form

e^{\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda|r_{i}|}\lambda^{-(2m-2-l)}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l)}f_{l}(\lambda;r_{i}),\quad\mbox{$l=0,\cdots,\frac{n-3}{2}$},

where

|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{r_{i}}^{\alpha}f_{l}(\lambda;r_{i})|\lesssim_{j,\alpha}\lambda^{-j}|r_{i}|^{-|\alpha|},\quad\lambda,|r_{i}|>0,\,\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n},

therefore
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
is a finite linear combination of the form

\begin{split}I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)=&\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})}\times\\
&\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}e^{\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda X}f(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V(x_{i})$}(1-\phi(\mbox{$\frac{X}{\delta T}$})){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}\right){\rm d}\lambda,\end{split}

(4.10)

where
\vec{l}=(l_{0},\cdots,l_{k})
with
l_{i}\in\{\mbox{$0,\cdots,\frac{n-3}{2}$}\}
, and

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{r_{0}}^{\alpha_{0}}\cdots\partial_{r_{k}}^{\alpha_{k}}f(\lambda;r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\right|\lesssim_{j,\alpha_{0},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}\lambda^{-j}|r_{0}|^{-|\alpha_{0}|}\cdots|r_{k}|^{-|\alpha_{k}|},\quad\lambda,|r_{i}|>0.

We split the discussion for
I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)
into two cases according to
\vec{l}
.

Case 1
:
There exists
i_{0}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
with

2m-2-l_{i}\geq 0,\quad i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i_{0}\}.

(4.11)

This case is irrelevant to the regularity of
V
, and somehow similar to that in
[
2
]
. For
j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
, let
D_{j}=\{(x_{1},\cdots,x_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{kn};\,|r_{j}|=\max_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|\}
, then
X\sim|r_{j}|
holds in
D_{j}
. We first rewrite (
4.10
)

\begin{split}I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)=&\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}\int_{D_{j}}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V(x_{i})$}(1-\phi(\mbox{$\frac{X}{\delta T}$}))\\
&\times\left(\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})}f(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k}){\rm d}\lambda\right){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}\\
:=&\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}I_{j}^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y),\end{split}

and it follows that

\begin{split}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})}f(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\in&S^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})}\left((\mbox{$\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2},+\infty$})\right)\\
\subset&S^{1+l_{i_{0}}}\left((\mbox{$\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2},+\infty$})\right),\end{split}

(4.12)

where the inclusion is due to assumption (
4.11
), and every relevant seminorm is bounded uniformly in parameters
r_{0},\cdots,r_{k}
by (
4.3
). Since
l_{i_{0}}\geq-1
implies
1+l_{i_{0}}\geq-\frac{1}{2}
, we apply Lemma
2.6
whenever
0<|t|\lesssim 1
or
|t|\gtrsim 1
to get

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})}f(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k}){\rm d}\lambda\right|\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}X^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}},\quad\mbox{$X\geq\frac{\delta T}{2}\gtrsim T$},\end{split}

where
\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}
is defined by (
2.6
). Consequently

\begin{split}&|I_{j}^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)|\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}\int_{\{X\gtrsim T\}\cap D_{j}}X^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}\langle x_{i}\rangle^{-\frac{n+1}{2}-}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}$}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}\\
\sim&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}\int_{\{X\gtrsim T\}\cap D_{j}}X^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}-(n-2-l_{j})}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}\langle x_{i}\rangle^{-\frac{n+1}{2}-}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{j\}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}$}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

where we note that

\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}+(n-2-l_{j})=\mbox{$\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}$}+\mbox{$\frac{1}{2m-1}(\frac{n-3}{2}-l_{i_{0}})$}+(\mbox{$\frac{n-3}{2}-l_{j}$})\geq 0.

(4.13)

To show long time estimate, we have when
X\sim|r_{j}|\gtrsim T\sim|t|\gtrsim 1
that

\begin{split}&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}X^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}-(n-2-l_{j})}\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}(|t|+|x-y|)^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}-\frac{n-1}{2}}\langle r_{j}\rangle^{-(\frac{n-3}{2}-l_{j})}\\
\sim&\langle r_{j}\rangle^{-(\frac{n-3}{2}-l_{j})}\begin{cases}|t|^{-\frac{n}{2}},\quad&|t|>|x-y|,\\
|t|^{-\frac{n}{2}+\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}-\frac{1}{2m-1}(\frac{n-3}{2}-l_{i_{0}})}|x-y|^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},&|t|\leq|x-y|,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{n}{2}}(1+|t|^{-1}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}}\langle r_{j}\rangle^{-(\frac{n-3}{2}-l_{j})},\end{split}

(4.14)

which implies when
|t|\gtrsim 1
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
that

\begin{split}|I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)|\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{n}{2}}(1+|t|^{-1}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}}\\
&\times\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\langle r_{j}\rangle^{-(\frac{n-3}{2}-l_{j})}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}\langle x_{i}\rangle^{-\frac{n+1}{2}-}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{j\}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}$}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}.\end{split}

The fact that the integrals above are bounded uniformly in
x_{0}
and
x_{k+1}
is a consequence of Lemma
2.3
if we first estimate the integral in
x_{j}
to get

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{j}\rangle^{-\frac{n-1}{2}-}{\rm d}x_{j}}{|x_{j-1}-x_{j}|^{n-2-l_{j-1}}\langle x_{j}-x_{j+1}\rangle^{\frac{n-3}{2}-l_{j}}}\lesssim 1,\quad|x_{j-1}-x_{j+1}|>0,

and then estimate the repeated integral in variables remained starting from
x_{j-1}
to
x_{1}
or from
x_{j+1}
to
x_{k}
.

To show short time estimate, recall (
4.13
), it follows when
0<|t|\lesssim 1
and
X\sim|r_{j}|\gtrsim T\sim|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}}
that, if
l_{i_{0}}\leq l_{j}
, we have

\begin{split}&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}X^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}-(n-2-l_{j})}\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}(|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}}+|x-y|)^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}-(n-2-l_{j})}\\
\sim&\begin{cases}|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}+\frac{1}{2m}(l_{j}-l_{i_{0}})},\quad&|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}}>|x-y|,\\
|t|^{-\frac{n}{2}+\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}+\frac{1}{2m}(l_{j}-l_{i_{0}})}|x-y|^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},&|t|^{\frac{1}{2m}}\leq|x-y|,\end{cases}\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\end{split}

(4.15)

and if
l_{i_{0}}>l_{j}
, we similarly have

\begin{split}|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}X^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}-(n-2-l_{j})}=&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}X^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}-(n-2-l_{i_{0}})}X^{-(l_{i_{0}}-l_{j})}\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}}X^{-\mu_{1+l_{i_{0}}}-(n-2-l_{i_{0}})}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-(l_{i_{0}}-l_{j})}\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-(l_{i_{0}}-l_{j})}.\end{split}

This implies when
0<|t|\lesssim 1
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
that

\begin{split}&|I_{j}^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)|\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}}\\
&\times\begin{cases}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}\langle x_{i}\rangle^{-\frac{n+1}{2}-}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{j\}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}$}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\quad&\text{if\,}l_{i_{0}}\leq l_{j},\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-(n-2-l_{j})}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}\langle x_{i}\rangle^{-\frac{n+1}{2}-}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{j,i_{0}\}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}$}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\quad&\text{if\,}l_{i_{0}}>l_{j},\end{cases}\end{split}

and the fact that the integrals above are bounded uniformly in
x_{0}
and
x_{k+1}
is also a consequence of Lemma
2.3
if we first estimate the integral in
x_{j}
to get

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{j}\rangle^{-\frac{n+1}{2}-}{\rm d}x_{j}}{|x_{j-1}-x_{j}|^{n-2-l_{j-1}}}\lesssim 1,\quad|x_{j-1}-x_{j}|>0,~\text{if}~i_{0}\neq j-1,

or

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{j}\rangle^{-\frac{n+1}{2}-}{\rm d}x_{j}}{|x_{j-1}-x_{j}|^{n-2-l_{j}}}\lesssim 1,\quad|x_{j-1}-x_{j}|>0,~\text{if}~i_{0}=j-1,

and then estimate the remained repeated integral in the same way.

Now we have proved

|I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)|\lesssim\begin{cases}|t|^{-\frac{n}{2}}(1+|t|^{-1}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad&|t|\gtrsim 1,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}(1+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2m}}|x-y|)^{-\frac{n(m-1)}{2m-1}},\quad&0<|t|\lesssim 1,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\end{cases}

under the assumption (
4.11
).

Case 2
:
There are at least two different indices
i_{1},i_{2}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
with

l_{i_{1}}+2-2m>0,\,l_{i_{2}}+2-2m>0.

(4.16)

This case is relevant to the regularity of
V
, and we need the whole next section to establish the complicated techniques.

5.
An integration by parts scheme for estimating
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)

5.1.
Heuristics

This section takes on our final task left at the end of the previous section, that is to obtain estimate (
4.11
) for
I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)
(defined by (
4.10
)) under the assumption (
4.16
), which completes the estimate (
4.5
) for
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
.

We first quickly give an impression of what we shall do in a rough sense. If we write

\begin{split}&U(\lambda,x_{0},x_{k+1},t)\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}e^{\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda X}f(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}$}\mbox{$\prod\limits_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V(x_{i})$}(1-\phi(\mbox{$\frac{X}{\delta T}$})){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.1)

then

I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})}U(\lambda,x_{0},x_{k+1},t){\rm d}\lambda.

Heuristically, we want to show that
U(\lambda,x_{0},x_{k+1},t)
can be written as a linear combination of the form

\lambda^{-J}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda X}g(\lambda,x_{0},\cdots,x_{k+1},t){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},

(5.2)

where
g
has bounded derivatives in
\lambda
for fixed
(x_{0},\cdots,x_{k+1},t)
, so that
I^{\vec{l}}(t;x,y)
can be written as a linear combination of the form

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}\left(\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})-J}g(\lambda,x_{0},\cdots,x_{k+1},t){\rm d}\lambda\right){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},

by changing the order of integration in the sense of oscillatory integral.

If
J
is large enough, which means the amplitude

\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})-J}g(\lambda,x_{0},\cdots,x_{k+1},t)

(5.3)

decays fast enough in
\lambda
, the mechanism run in Section
4.3
would first implies a good estimate at least in
t
for the integral in
\lambda
, for example a bound like

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\mathrm{i}t\lambda^{2m}\pm\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{\chi}(\lambda^{2m})\lambda^{2m-1-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}(2m-2-l_{i})-J}g(\lambda,x_{0},\cdots,x_{k+1},t){\rm d}\lambda\right|\\
\lesssim&|t|^{-\frac{n}{2m}}G(x_{0},\cdots,x_{k+1},t),\end{split}

(5.4)

which intuitively leads to a correct dispersive type estimate, so that we can further hope the integration of
G(x_{0},\cdots,x_{k+1})
in
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{k})
to give the spatial counterpart of the estimate in (
4.11
).

There will be two major difficulties when bootstrapping the above heuristics:

(i)

If
\lambda^{-J}
in (
5.2
) comes from integrating (
5.1
) by parts
J
times in
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{k})
, then
V
is of course required to have some regularity. In an extreme case where
l_{0}=\cdots=l_{k}=\frac{n-3}{2}
, referring to (
4.12
) in the previous section, if we want (
5.3
) to lie in
S^{\frac{n-1}{2}}((\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2},+\infty))
, then we need
J=-k(2m-2-\frac{n-3}{2})=k(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)
, which roughly means averagely we have to integrate by parts
\frac{n+1}{2}-2m
times in every
x_{i}
(
i=1,\cdots,k
), and the regularity
V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}
is necessary for such purpose. The first difficulty is to show that why
V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}
is actually sufficient, as expected in the Introduction that such regularity condition is possibly the sharp one.

(ii)

If
g
has bounded derivatives in
\lambda
for fixed
(x_{0},\cdots,x_{k+1},t)
, then the oscillatory integral estimate (
5.4
) has little to do with
g
, and the behavior of
G
in variables
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{k})
should be consistent with
g
. On the other hand,
g
is at least as singular in
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{k})
as the integrand in (
5.1
), and so is
G
. However, besides the already existing point singularities
\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}
, we will see that
G
or
g
can also have singularities at some line segments, which are introduced when integrating (
5.1
) by parts, while in fact more point singularities will also be introduced. The second difficulty is to show that why such mixture of point and line singularities are so integrable to give desired estimates.

In the rest of this section, we will rigorously establish an integration by parts scheme to serve the above heuristics. There two main technical results. The first one that explains (
5.2
) in details is Proposition
5.1
. The second one that explains the estimate of the above
G
is Proposition
5.19
. We will finally apply these two results to complete the estimate of
\Omega_{k}^{high,2}(t,x,y)
in Section
5.5
.

5.2.
The integration by parts scheme

Recall
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\mathbb{K}=\{1,\cdots,k\}
and
\mathbb{K}_{0}=\{0,\cdots,k\}
, we consider the oscillatory integral in the form of

\begin{split}U^{\vec{l}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i})}f(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\phi(X/T){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

where
r_{i}=x_{i}-x_{i+1}
,
X=|r_{0}|+\cdots+|r_{k}|
,
\vec{l}=\{l_{1},\cdots,l_{k}\}
with
0\leq l_{i}\leq\frac{n-3}{2}
,
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is bounded with
\phi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
T>0
,
V\in C^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies (
1.4
), and
f
satisfies
