\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{r_{0}}^{\alpha_{0}}\cdots\partial_{r_{k}}^{\alpha_{k}}f(\lambda;r_{0},\cdots,r_{k})\right|\lesssim_{j,\alpha_{0},\cdots,\alpha_{k}}\lambda^{-j}|r_{0}|^{-|\alpha_{0}|}\cdots|r_{k}|^{-|\alpha_{k}|},\quad\lambda,|r_{i}|>0.

Below are the assumptions and notations throughout the rest of this section.

•

Assume (
4.16
), i.e. there are at least two different indices
i_{1},i_{2}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
such that

l_{i_{1}}+2-2m>0,\,l_{i_{2}}+2-2m>0.

(5.5)

•

Let
\sigma
be a fixed permutation of
\mathbb{K}_{0}
such that
L_{k}\geq L_{k-1}\geq\cdots\geq L_{0}
where

L_{i}=\max\{0,l_{\sigma(i)}+2-2m\},\quad i\in\mathbb{K}_{0},

(5.6)

and we define
k_{0}=\min\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};\,L_{i}>0\}
.

•

If
A
is a non-empty finite subset of
\mathbb{Z}
, we define

\begin{split}N(A,i)&=\min\{j\in A;\,j\geq i\},\quad i\leq\max A,\\
L(A,i)&=\max\{j\in A;\,j<i\},\quad i>\min A.\end{split}

(5.7)

•

If
A
is a finite subset of
\mathbb{Z}
, we define

D_{i}A=\begin{cases}A\setminus\{N(A,i)\},\quad&A\neq\emptyset~\text{and}~i\leq\max A,\\
A,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(5.8)

One checks that
D_{i}D_{j}A=D_{j}D_{i}A
always holds, so it is reasonable to denote

D_{I}A=\left(\prod_{i\in I}D_{i}\right)A,\quad I\subset\mathbb{Z},

(5.9)

and it is also true that
D_{I_{1}}D_{I_{2}}A=D_{I_{2}}D_{I_{1}}A
for any
I_{1},I_{2}\subset\mathbb{Z}
, but it may not be equal to
\prod_{i\in I_{1}\cup I_{2}}D_{i}A
if
I_{1}\cap I_{2}\neq\emptyset
. It obviously follows that
D_{I_{1}}A\subset D_{I_{2}}B
if
I_{1}\supset I_{2}
and
A\subset B
.

•

Denoted by

E_{i,j}=\frac{x_{i}-x_{i+1}}{|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|}-\frac{x_{j}-x_{j+1}}{|x_{j}-x_{j+1}|},\quad i,j\in\mathbb{K}_{0},\,i\neq j,

(5.10)

if
F
is a non-empty finite set of
E_{i,j}
with
i<j
, we define the norm of
F
to be

\|F\|=\left(\sum_{E_{i,j}\in F}|E_{i,j}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.11)

If
F=\{E_{i_{1},j_{1}},\cdots,E_{i_{r},j_{r}}\}
with
j_{1}<\cdots<j_{r}
, we sometimes interpret
F
to be the vector
F=(E_{i_{1},j_{1}},\cdots,E_{i_{r},j_{r}})\in\mathbb{R}^{rn}
for convenience.

Now we introduce our first main technical result that will be used in Section
5.5
when studying a specific type of oscillatory integrals.

Proposition 5.1
.

For every
\mu\in\{1,\cdots,k-k_{0}\}
,
U^{\vec{l}}
is a finite linear combination of oscillatory integrals in the form of

\begin{split}\lambda^{-J}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\alpha_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+d_{i})}\prod_{i=1}^{s}\|F_{i}\|^{-{p_{i}}}\\
&\times g(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k},F_{1},\cdots,F_{s})\psi(X/T){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.12)

and every such integral is equipped with two sequences of indices

\begin{split}\emptyset=&I_{0,1}^{*}\subset\cdots\subset I_{s,1}^{*}=\{\mbox{$i\in\mathbb{K};\,|\alpha_{i}|=\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}\},\\
\left\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};\,l_{i}+2-2m\leq 0\right\}=&I_{0,2}^{*}\subset\cdots\subset I_{s,2}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};\,d_{i}=\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\}\},\end{split}

(5.13)

satisfying the following constraints:

(1)

J=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}}|\alpha_{i}|+\sum_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}d_{i}
, and it follows that

\begin{cases}1\leq s\leq\mu,\\
|\alpha_{i}|\leq\frac{n+1}{2}-2m,\quad&i\in\mathbb{K},\\
0\leq d_{i}\leq\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},\quad&i\in\mathbb{K}_{0},\\
L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k_{0}+\mu-1}\leq J\leq L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}.\\
\end{cases}

(5.14)

If
s<\mu
, it further follows that
J=L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}
.

(2)

If
J<L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}
, then for
i=1,\cdots,s
, there exists
\tau^{(i)}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
for either

\begin{cases}I_{i,1}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,1}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i)}\}\\
I_{i,2}^{*}=I_{i-1,2}^{*}\end{cases}\quad\text{or}\quad\begin{cases}I_{i,1}^{*}=I_{i-1,1}^{*}\\
I_{i,2}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i)}\}\end{cases}

(5.15)

to hold. If
J=L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}
,
s\geq 2
and
1\leq i\leq s-1
, such
\tau^{(i)}
also exists.

(3)

Denoted by

I_{i}^{*}=D_{I_{i,2}^{*}}(\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{i,1}^{*})=D_{I_{i,2}^{*}}D_{I_{i,1}^{*}}\mathbb{K}=D_{I_{i,1}^{*}}D_{I_{i,2}^{*}}\mathbb{K},\quad i=0,\cdots,s,

(5.16)

it follows that
I_{i-1}^{*}\neq\emptyset
whenever
\tau^{(i)}
exists.

(4)

Denoted by

F_{i}=\left\{E_{j_{1},j_{2}};\,j_{2}\in I_{i-1}^{*},\,j_{1}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j_{2})\right\},\quad i=1,\cdots,s,

(5.17)

it follows that

p_{i}+\cdots+p_{s}\leq\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}2d_{j},\quad i=1,\cdots,s,

(5.18)

where

\begin{split}I_{F_{i}}^{1}&=\{j\in\mathbb{K};\,j_{1}<j\leq j_{2},~E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{i}\},\\
I_{F_{i}}^{2}&=\{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0};\,E_{j,j^{\prime}}~\text{or}~E_{j^{\prime},j}\in F_{i}\}.\end{split}

(5.19)

It also follows for
i=1,\cdots,s
that

I_{i,1}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,1}^{*}\subset I_{F_{i}}^{1},\quad I_{i,2}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}\subset I_{F_{i}}^{2}.

(5.20)

(5)

g\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{1+(k+1)n+\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}(k-k_{0}-i)n}\setminus\{0\})
satisfies

|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{r_{0}}^{\alpha_{0}}\cdots\partial_{r_{k}}^{\alpha_{k}}\partial_{F_{1}}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots\partial_{F_{s}}^{\beta_{s}}g|\lesssim\lambda^{-j}|r_{0}|^{-|\alpha_{0}|}\cdots|r_{k}|^{-|\alpha_{k}|}\|F_{1}\|^{-|\beta_{1}|}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-|\beta_{s}|}.

(5.21)

We also have
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
bounded and
\mathrm{supp}\psi\subset\mathrm{supp}\phi
.

Remark 5.2
.

In the third constraint, that
I_{i-1}^{*}\neq\emptyset
whenever
\tau^{(i)}
exists is a consequence of the second constraint, because
i-1<s
always holds in such cases, and we know by
\#\,I_{0,1}^{*}=0
and
\#\,I_{0,2}^{*}=k_{0}
that

\#I_{i-1}^{*}=k-k_{0}-(i-1)>k-k_{0}-s\geq k-k_{0}-\mu\geq 0.

If
\mu<k-k_{0}
and
J<L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}
, we then have
I_{s}^{*}\neq\emptyset
since
\tau^{(s)}
must exist and so

\#I_{s}^{*}=k-k_{0}-s\geq k-k_{0}-\mu\geq 1.

Remark 5.3
.

That
F_{i}
in (
5.17
) is well defined is a consequence of
I_{i-1}^{*}\neq\emptyset
, which holds in the context for
\#I_{0}^{*}=k-k_{0}\geq 1
and the other cases are explained in Remark
5.2
. To see this, we first note that
\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}\neq\emptyset
for it contains the non-empty
I_{i-1}^{*}
. If
j=\min I_{i-1}^{*}
, it follows that
j>\min(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*})
, for otherwise we must have
\{0,\cdots,j-1\}\subset I_{i-1,2}^{*}
, and then
I_{i-1}^{*}\subset D_{I_{i-1,2}^{*}}\mathbb{K}\subset D_{\{0,\cdots,j-1\}}\mathbb{K}=\mathbb{K}\setminus\{1,\cdots,j\}
yields a contradiction. Therefore
j_{1}
is well defined for each
j_{2}\in I_{i-1}^{*}
in (
5.17
). If
E_{j_{1},j_{2}},E_{j_{3},j_{4}}\in F_{i}
and
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\neq E_{j_{3},j_{4}}
, it is easy to check that either
j_{1}<j_{2}\leq j_{3}<j_{4}
or
j_{3}<j_{4}\leq j_{1}<j_{2}
must hold, so
\|F_{i}\|
is also well defined.

Remark 5.4
.

It follows by definition that

\begin{split}I_{F_{i}}^{1}=&\bigcup_{j\in I_{i-1}^{*}}\{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j)+1,\cdots,j\},\\
I_{F_{i}}^{2}=&\bigcup_{j\in I_{i-1}^{*}}\{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j),j\}.\end{split}

(5.22)

Therefore (
5.20
) implies that

\tau^{(i)}\leq\max I_{i-1}^{*},

(5.23)

holds whenever
\tau^{(i)}
defined in constraint 3) exists.

Lemma 5.5
.

If integral (
5.12
) in Proposition
5.1
is given with
s\geq 2
, then

I_{F_{i}}^{1}\supset I_{F_{i+1}}^{1},\,I_{F_{i}}^{2}\supset I_{F_{i+1}}^{2},\,\|F_{i}\|\geq\|F_{i+1}\|,\quad i=1,\cdots,s-1.

(5.24)

The conclusion also holds for
i=s
if
\mu<k-k_{0}
and
J<L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}
, while
I_{s}^{*}\neq\emptyset
by Remark
5.2
and then
F_{s+1}
can be defined in the way of (
5.17
) by Remark
5.3
.

Proof.

We first note that the conclusion holds in the special case
F_{i+1}\subset F_{i}
. Also note that when
s\geq 2
and
1\leq i\leq s-1
, we always have the existence of
\tau^{(i)}
, and
I_{i}^{*}=D_{\tau^{(i)}}I_{i-1}^{*}
holds by constraint 2), meaning
I_{i}^{*}\subset I_{i-1}^{*}
and

I_{i-1}^{*}\setminus I_{i}^{*}=\{N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})\},

(5.25)

where
N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})
is well defined because of (
5.23
). By Remark
5.2
, (
5.25
) is also true for
i=s
if
\mu<k-k_{0}
and
J<L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}
.

If
I_{i,1}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,1}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i)}\}
and
I_{i,2}^{*}=I_{i-1,2}^{*}
, we of course have
F_{i+1}\subset F_{i}
by definition.

If
I_{i,1}^{*}=I_{i-1,1}^{*}
,
I_{i,2}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i)}\}
, and
\tau^{(i)}=\max I_{i-1}^{*}
, then
\tau^{(i)}=N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})
by definition. For any
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{i+1}
, we have
j_{2}\in I_{i}^{*}\subset I_{i-1}^{*}
,
j_{2}<\tau^{(i)}
and

j_{1}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus(I_{i-1,2}^{*}\cup\{\tau^{(i)}\}),j_{2})=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j_{2}),

(5.26)

which means
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{i}
. So
F_{i+1}\subset F_{i}
is also true.

If
I_{i,1}^{*}=I_{i-1,1}^{*}
,
I_{i,2}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i)}\}
,
\tau^{(i)}<\max I_{i-1}^{*}
and
\tau^{(i)}\notin I_{i-1}^{*}
, we must have
\tau^{(i)}\notin I_{i}^{*}
and
\tau^{(i)}<N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})
. So for any
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{i+1}
, we have either
j_{2}<\tau^{(i)}
or
j_{2}>N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})
. Now (
5.26
) of course holds when
j_{2}<\tau^{(i)}
, and it also holds when
j_{2}>N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})
because
\tau^{(i)}<N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})\in I_{i-1}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}
, which combined with the assumption
I_{i,2}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i)}\}
also implies
N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})\in I_{i-1}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i,2}^{*}
. Therefore
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{i}
, and
F_{i+1}\subset F_{i}
still holds.

Lastly, if
I_{i,1}^{*}=I_{i-1,1}^{*}
,
I_{i,2}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i)}\}
,
\tau^{(i)}<\max I_{i-1}^{*}
and
\tau^{(i)}\in I_{i-1}^{*}
, we must have
\tau^{(i)}=N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})
and the existence of
N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)=N(I_{i}^{*},\tau^{(i)})\in I_{i}^{*}
. Splitting
F_{i}=F_{i}^{(1)}\cup F_{i}^{(2)}
where

\begin{split}F_{i}^{(1)}=\{E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{i};\,j_{2}<\tau^{(i)}\,\text{or}\,j_{2}>N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)\},\\
F_{i}^{(2)}=\{E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{i};\,j_{2}=\tau^{(i)}\,\text{or}\,j_{2}=N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)\},\end{split}

(5.27)

the same discussion in the previous case shows that

F_{i}^{(1)}=\{E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{i+1};\,j_{2}<\tau^{(i)}\,\text{or}\,j_{2}>N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)\},

(5.28)

and therefore

F_{i+1}=F_{i}^{(1)}\cup\{E_{\tilde{j},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)}\},

(5.29)

where
\tilde{j}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i,2}^{*},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1))
. If

L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1))>\tau^{(i)},

we must have

L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1))=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus(I_{i-1,2}^{*}\cup\{\tau^{(i)}\}),N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1))=\tilde{j},

which means
E_{\tilde{j},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)}\in F_{i}
and therefore
F_{i+1}\subset F_{i}
. If

L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1))=\tau^{(i)},

(5.30)

which is the only possibility left since
N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)>\tau^{(i)}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}
, we must have

\tilde{j}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus(I_{i-1,2}^{*}\cup\{\tau^{(i)}\}),N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1))<\tau^{(i)},

and consequently

\tilde{j}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i,2}^{*},\tau^{(i)})=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus(I_{i-1,2}^{*}\cup\{\tau^{(i)}\}),\tau^{(i)})=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},\tau^{(i)}),

which together with (
5.27
) and (
5.30
) implies

F_{i}=F_{i}^{(1)}\cup\{E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},\tau^{(i)}),\tau^{(i)}},E_{\tau^{(i)},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)}\}=F_{i}^{(1)}\cup\{E_{\tilde{j},\tau^{(i)}},E_{\tau^{(i)},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)}\},

(5.31)

so it is now obvious to see
I_{F_{i}}^{1}=I_{F_{i+1}}^{1}
and
I_{F_{i}}^{2}\supset I_{F_{i+1}}^{2}
if we compare (
5.29
) with (
5.31
), while
\|F_{i}\|\geq\|F_{i+1}\|
is a consequence of the triangle inequality applied to
E_{\tilde{j},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)}=E_{\tilde{j},\tau^{(i)}}+E_{\tau^{(i)},N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)}+1)}
.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

The proof also shows that either
F_{i+1}\subset F_{i}
holds, or there exist
j_{1}<j_{2}<j_{3}
such that
E_{j_{1},j_{2}},E_{j_{2},j_{3}}\in F_{i}
,
E_{j_{1},j_{3}}\in F_{i+1}
and
F_{i}\setminus\{E_{j_{1},j_{2}},E_{j_{2},j_{3}}\}=F_{i+1}\setminus\{E_{j_{1},j_{3}}\}
.

Next, we introduce some lemmas for the proof of Proposition
5.1
, and formally the key one is Lemma
5.9
which exploits the pattern if we integrate (
5.12
) by parts once and once again.

Lemma 5.7
.

If integral (
5.12
) in Proposition
5.1
is given, then

I_{F_{i}}^{1}\subset\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{i-1,1}^{*},~I_{F_{i}}^{2}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},\quad i=1,\cdots,s.

The conclusion also holds for
i=s+1
if
I_{s}^{*}\neq\emptyset
while
F_{s+1}
can be defined in the way of (
5.17
) by Remark
5.3
.

Proof.

To show the first inclusion, we pick up any
j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}
, then there exists
i_{0}\in I_{i-1}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}
with

L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},i_{0})<j\leq i_{0},

which implies
D_{j}(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*})=(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*})\setminus\{i_{0}\}
, so it is impossible for
j\in I_{i-1,1}^{*}
to hold, because otherwise

I_{i-1}^{*}=D_{I_{i-1,1}^{*}}D_{I_{i-1,2}^{*}}\mathbb{K}\subset D_{j}(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*})=(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*})\setminus\{i_{0}\}\notni i_{0},

which is a contradiction. The second inclusion is obvious by (
5.22
).
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Given
I_{1}\subset\mathbb{K}
,
I_{2}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}
,
I=D_{I_{2}}(\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{1})\neq\emptyset
,
y_{0}=x_{0}
,
y_{k+1}=x_{k+1}
and the change of variables

y_{i}=\begin{cases}x_{i}-x_{i+1},\quad&i\in\mathbb{K}\cap I_{2},\\
x_{i},&i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{2}.\end{cases}

Then for each
i\in I
, the following statements hold:

1)

If
j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
,
j\neq i
and
j\neq L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)
, then
x_{j}-x_{j+1}
is independent of
y_{i}
in the
y
-coordinates.

2)

\nabla_{y_{i}}X=-E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i),i}
holds where
E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i),i}
is defined.

Proof.

For each
i\in I
, we first note that
L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)
is well defined for the same reason explained in Remark
5.3
. To show 1), one checks that

x_{j}-x_{j+1}=\begin{cases}y_{j},&j\in\mathbb{K}\cap I_{2},\\
y_{j}-\sum_{\tau=j+1}^{j^{*}}y_{\tau},\quad&j\in(\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{2})\cup\{0\},\end{cases}

where

j^{*}=N((\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{2})\cup\{k+1\},j+1).

The case of
j\in\mathbb{K}\cap I_{2}
is obvious because
I\subset\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{2}
. If
j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus(\mathbb{K}\cap I_{2})
,
j\neq i
and
j\neq L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)
, since
L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus(\mathbb{K}\cap I_{2}),i)
always holds, we have either
j<j+1\leq\cdots\leq j^{*}<i
or
i<j<j+1\leq\cdots\leq j^{*}
, and therefore
x_{j}-x_{j+1}=y_{j}-\sum_{\tau=j+1}^{j^{*}}y_{\tau}
is also independent of
y_{i}
.

To show 2), by the conclusion of 1) and the fact that
L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)^{*}=i
, we derive

\begin{split}\nabla_{y_{i}}X&=\nabla_{y_{i}}|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|+\nabla_{y_{i}}|x_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)}-x_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)+1}|\\
&=\nabla_{y_{i}}\left|\mbox{$y_{i}-\sum_{\tau=i+1}^{i^{*}}y_{\tau}$}\right|+\nabla_{y_{i}}\left|y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}\right|\\
&=\frac{\mbox{$y_{i}-\sum_{\tau=i+1}^{i^{*}}y_{\tau}$}}{\left|\mbox{$y_{i}-\sum_{\tau=i+1}^{i^{*}}y_{\tau}$}\right|}-\frac{y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}}{\left|y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}\right|}\\
&=\frac{x_{i}-x_{i+1}}{|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|}-\frac{x_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)}-x_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)+1}}{|x_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)}-x_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i)+1}|}\\
&=-E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{2},i),i}.\end{split}

(5.32)

∎

Lemma 5.9
.

Given
\dot{s}\in\{0,\cdots,k-k_{0}-1\}
,
\dot{\alpha}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
for
i\in\mathbb{K}
,
\dot{d}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
for
i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
, and two sequences of indices

I_{0,1}^{*}\subset\cdots\subset I_{\dot{s},1}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K},\quad I_{0,2}^{*}\subset\cdots\subset I_{\dot{s},2}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0},

(5.33)

with
I_{i}^{*}\neq\emptyset
for
i=0,\cdots,\dot{s}
where
I_{i}^{*}
is defined in the way of (
5.16
) so that
F_{1},\cdots,F_{\dot{s}+1}
can be defined in the way of (
5.17
) by Remark
5.3
, and we assume

\begin{cases}I_{F_{1}}^{1}\supset\cdots\supset I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{1},\,I_{F_{1}}^{2}\supset\cdots\supset I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2},\\
|\dot{\alpha}_{i}|\leq\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}$}-2m,&i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{1},\\
0\leq\dot{d}_{i}\leq\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},&i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2},\\
|\dot{\alpha}_{i}|<\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$},&i\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{1},\\
0\leq\dot{d}_{i}<\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},&i\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2},\end{cases}

(5.34)

where
I_{F_{i}}^{1}
and
I_{F_{i}}^{2}
are defined in the way of (
5.19
) which is also equivalent to (
5.22
). Consider the expression

\begin{split}\lambda^{-\dot{J}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\dot{d}_{i})}\prod_{i=1}^{\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\\
&\times\dot{g}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k},F_{1},\cdots,F_{\dot{s}+1})\dot{\psi}(X/T){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.35)

where
\dot{J}=|\dot{\alpha}_{1}|+\cdots+|\dot{\alpha}_{k}|+\dot{d}_{0}+\cdots+\dot{d}_{k}
,
\dot{g}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k},F_{1},\cdots,F_{\dot{s}+1})
is smooth and supported away from the origin in every variable satisfying estimates of the same type to (
5.21
),
\dot{\psi}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is bounded with
\dot{\psi}^{\prime}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, and
\dot{p}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with

\dot{p}_{i}+\cdots+\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}\leq\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\dot{\alpha}_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}2\dot{d}_{j},\quad i=1,\cdots,\dot{s}+1.

(5.36)

Then integral (
5.35
) is a finite linear combination of the form

\begin{split}\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\ddot{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\ddot{d}_{i})}\prod_{i=1}^{\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\ddot{p}_{i}}}\\
&\times\ddot{g}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k},F_{1},\cdots,F_{\dot{s}+1})\ddot{\psi}(X/T){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.37)

where
\ddot{g}
and
\ddot{\psi}
inherit the properties of
\dot{g}
and
\dot{\psi}
respectively, and furthermore, there either exists
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{1}
such that

\begin{cases}|\ddot{\alpha}_{i_{0}}|=|\dot{\alpha}_{i_{0}}|+1,\\
\ddot{\alpha}_{i}=\dot{\alpha}_{i},\quad&i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{i_{0}\},\\
\ddot{d}_{i}=\dot{d}_{i},&i\in\mathbb{K}_{0},\end{cases}

(5.38)

or exists
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2}
such that

\begin{cases}\ddot{d}_{i_{0}}=\dot{d}_{i_{0}}+1,\\
\ddot{\alpha}_{i}=\dot{\alpha}_{i},\quad&i\in\mathbb{K},\\
\ddot{d}_{i}=\dot{d}_{i},&i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i_{0}\},\end{cases}

(5.39)

while in both cases we always have

\ddot{p}_{i}+\cdots+\ddot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}\leq\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\ddot{\alpha}_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}2\ddot{d}_{j},\quad i=1,\cdots,\dot{s}+1.

(5.40)

Proof.

Set
y_{0}=x_{0}
,
y_{k+1}=x_{k+1}
, and the change of variables

y_{i}=\begin{cases}x_{i}-x_{i+1},\quad&i\in\mathbb{K}\cap I_{\dot{s},2}^{*}\\
x_{i},&i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},\end{cases}

we have

r_{i}=x_{i}-x_{i+1}=\begin{cases}y_{i},&i\in\mathbb{K}\cap I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},\\
y_{i}-\sum_{\tau=i+1}^{i^{*}}y_{\tau},\quad&i\in(\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*})\cup\{0\},\end{cases}

where
i^{*}=N((\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*})\cup\{k+1\},i+1)
, and

x_{i}=\sum_{\tau=i}^{(i-1)^{*}}y_{\tau},\quad i\in\mathbb{K},

so such change of variables and its inverse only leave with universal constants. We denote
\tilde{V}=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})
,
\tilde{r}=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\dot{d}_{i})}
,
\tilde{g}=\dot{g}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k},F_{1},\cdots,F_{\dot{s}+1})
and
\tilde{\psi}=\dot{\psi}(X/T)
for convention.

In the sequel, we will frequently use Lemma
5.8
with
I_{1}=I_{\dot{s},1}^{*}
,
I_{2}=I_{\dot{s},2}^{*}
and so
I=I_{\dot{s}}^{*}\neq\emptyset
. Let
\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}=(\nabla_{y_{i_{1}}},\cdots,\nabla_{y_{i_{\nu}}})
where
i_{1},\cdots,i_{\nu}\in I_{\dot{s}}^{*}
be increasing. The second conclusion of Lemma
5.8
shows that

\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X=-\left(E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i_{1}),i_{1}},\cdots,E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i_{\nu}),i_{\nu}}\right),

(5.41)

and therefore
|\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X|=\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|
. Note that

e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}=\mathrm{i}^{-1}\lambda^{-1}|\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X|^{-2}\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X\cdot\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}=\mathrm{i}^{-1}\lambda^{-1}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-2}\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X\cdot\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X},

integration by parts in the
y
-coordinates gives

-\mathrm{i}^{-1}\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}\cdot\left(\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}\tilde{V}\tilde{r}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+2)}\prod_{i=1}^{\dot{s}}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X\right){\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k},

(5.42)

and there are five types of integrals derived from (
5.42
).

The first type is

\mathrm{I}=\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}\tilde{r}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\prod_{i=1}^{\dot{s}}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\left(\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}\tilde{V}\cdot\frac{\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X}{\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|}\right){\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}.

Note that

\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}\tilde{V}=\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}\mbox{$\left(\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(\sum_{\tau=i}^{(i-1)^{*}}y_{\tau})\right)$},

(5.43)

if
i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{1}
, it must follows that
\{i,\cdots,(i-1)^{*}\}\cap I_{\dot{s}}^{*}=\emptyset
, otherwise there exists
j\in\{i,\cdots,(i-1)^{*}\}\cap I_{\dot{s}}^{*}\subset\{i,\cdots,N((\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*})\cup\{k+1\},i)\}\cap(\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*})
, and then
j=N((\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*})\cup\{k+1\},i)=N(\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)
holds, which implies
L(\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},j)<i\leq j
and the contradiction
i\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{1}
. So the gradient in (
5.43
) only falls on
V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i_{0}})}(\sum_{\tau=i_{0}}^{(i_{0}-1)^{*}}y_{\tau})
where
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{1}
. On the other hand, (
5.41
) indicates that each one dimensional component of
\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X/\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|
is a component of
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}/\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|
for some
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{\dot{s}+1}
. We thus conclude that
\mathrm{I}
is a finite linear combination of

\begin{split}&\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{r}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\times V^{(\ddot{\alpha}_{i_{0}})}(\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=i_{0}}^{(i_{0}-1)^{*}}y_{\tau}$})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{i_{0}\}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=i}^{(i-1)^{*}}y_{\tau}$})\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}Q{\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}\\
=&C\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}V^{(\ddot{\alpha}_{i_{0}})}(x_{i_{0}})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{i_{0}\}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\dot{d}_{i})}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}Q{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.44)

where
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{1}
,
|\ddot{\alpha}_{i_{0}}|=|\dot{\alpha}_{i_{0}}|+1
, and
Q
is a monomial in
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}/\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|
for some
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{\dot{s}+1}
.

The second type of integrals derived from (
5.42
) is

\mathrm{II}=\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}\tilde{V}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\left(\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}\tilde{r}\cdot\frac{\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X}{\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|}\right){\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}.

If
i\in I_{\dot{s}}^{*}
and
i_{0}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
, Lemma
5.8
indicates that
y_{i}
is independent of
r_{i_{0}}
unless
i_{0}=i
or
i_{0}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)
, where in either case we have
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2}
by definition, and of course
i_{0}\in(\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*})\cup\{0\}
. One checks in a way similar to (
5.32
) that

\nabla_{y_{i}}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-1}=\begin{cases}-|r_{i_{0}}|^{-2}\frac{r_{i_{0}}}{|r_{i_{0}}|},\quad&i_{0}=i,\\
|r_{i_{0}}|^{-2}\frac{r_{i_{0}}}{|r_{i_{0}}|},&i_{0}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),\end{cases}

so we conclude that
\mathrm{II}
is a finite linear combination of

\begin{split}&\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{V}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\times|r_{i_{0}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i_{0}}+\dot{d}_{i_{0}}+1)}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i_{0}\}}|r_{i}|^{-n-2-l_{i}+\dot{d}_{i}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}Q{\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}\\
=&C\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i_{0}}+\dot{d}_{i_{0}}+1)}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i_{0}\}}|r_{i}|^{-n-2-l_{i}+\dot{d}_{i}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\times\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}Q{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.45)

where
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2}
and
Q
is a polynomial in
(r_{i_{0}}/|r_{i_{0}}|,E_{j_{1},j_{2}}/\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|)
for some
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{\dot{s}+1}
.

The third type of integrals derived from (
5.42
) is

\mathrm{III}=\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}\tilde{V}\tilde{r}\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}\left(\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+2)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\right)\cdot\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X{\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}.

If
i\in I_{\dot{s}}^{*}
and
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}
, by Lemma
5.8
,
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}
is independent of
y_{i}
unless

\{j_{1},j_{2}\}\cap\{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),i\}\neq\emptyset,

so then for
i\in I_{\dot{s}}^{*}
and
1\leq j_{0}\leq\dot{s}+1
, we have

\nabla_{y_{i}}\|F_{j_{0}}\|^{-1}=-\mbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\|F_{j_{0}}\|^{-3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{j_{0}}\\
\{j_{1},j_{2}\}\cap\{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),i\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\nabla_{y_{i}}\left|E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\right|^{2},

and there are at most three non-trivial terms in the summation since
L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)<i
:

•

If
j_{1}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)
and
j_{2}\neq i
, then
y_{i}
is independent of
r_{j_{2}}
by Lemma
5.8
, and similar to (
5.32
), we have

\begin{split}\nabla_{y_{i}}\left|E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\right|^{2}=&\nabla_{y_{i}}\left|\frac{y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}}{|y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}|}-\frac{r_{j_{2}}}{|r_{j_{2}}|}\right|^{2}\\
=&-2\left(E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),j_{2}}\cdot r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}\right)\frac{r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}}{|r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}|^{3}}-2\frac{E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),j_{2}}}{|r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}|}.\end{split}

•

If
j_{1}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)
and
j_{2}=i
, then

\begin{split}\nabla_{y_{i}}\left|E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\right|^{2}=&\nabla_{y_{i}}\left|\frac{y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}}{|y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}|}-\frac{\mbox{$y_{i}-\sum_{\tau=i+1}^{i^{*}}y_{\tau}$}}{|\mbox{$y_{i}-\sum_{\tau=i+1}^{i^{*}}y_{\tau}$}|}\right|^{2}\\
=&-2\left(E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),i}\cdot r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}\right)\frac{r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}}{|r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}|^{3}}+2\left(E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),i}\cdot r_{i}\right)\frac{r_{i}}{|r_{i}|^{3}}\\
&-2\left(|r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}|^{-1}+|r_{i}|^{-1}\right)E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),i}.\end{split}

•

If
j_{2}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)
, then
y_{i}
is independent of
r_{j_{1}}
, and calculation in the first case above implies

\nabla_{y_{i}}\left|E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\right|^{2}=2\left(E_{j_{1},L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}\cdot r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}\right)\frac{r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}}{|r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}|^{3}}+2\frac{E_{j_{1},L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}}{|r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}|}.

Since
I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2}=\cup_{i\in I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}\{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),i\}
, the above argument implies that when
1\leq j_{0}\leq\dot{s}+1
, each one dimensional component of
\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}\|F_{j_{0}}\|^{-1}
is a sum of the form
\|F_{j_{0}}\|^{-2}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-1}Q
where
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2}
and
Q
is a polynomial in
(r_{i_{0}}/|r_{i_{0}}|,E_{j_{1},j_{2}}/\|F_{j_{0}}\|)
for some
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{j_{0}}
. Hence if
\dot{s}\geq 1
,
\mathrm{III}
is then a finite linear combination of

\begin{split}&\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{V}\tilde{r}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-1}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+2)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}Q_{1}{\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}\\
=&C\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i_{0}}+\dot{d}_{i_{0}}+1)}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i_{0}\}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\dot{d}_{i})}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+2)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}Q_{1}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.46)

and

\begin{split}\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{V}\tilde{r}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-1}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\|F_{j_{0}}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{j_{0}}+1)}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i<\dot{s}+1\\
i\neq j_{0}\end{subarray}}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}Q_{2}{\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}\\
=C\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i_{0}}+\dot{d}_{i_{0}}+1)}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i_{0}\}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\dot{d}_{i})}\\
&\times\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\|F_{j_{0}}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{j_{0}}+1)}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i<\dot{s}+1\\
i\neq j_{0}\end{subarray}}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}Q_{2}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.47)

where
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2}
,
Q_{1}
is a polynomial in
(r_{i_{0}}/|r_{i_{0}}|,E_{j_{1},j_{2}}/\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|)
for some
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{\dot{s}+1}
,
1\leq j_{0}\leq\dot{s}
, and
Q_{2}
is a polynomial in
(r_{i_{0}}/|r_{i_{0}}|,E_{j_{1},j_{2}}/\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|,E_{j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}}/\|F_{j_{0}}\|)
for some
E_{j_{1},j_{2}}\in F_{\dot{s}+1}
and some
E_{j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}}\in F_{j_{0}}
. If
\dot{s}=0
, we then only have terms like (
5.46
) in the combination.

The fourth type of integrals derived from (
5.42
) is

\mathrm{IV}=\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}(\Delta_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X)\tilde{V}\tilde{r}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+2)}\prod_{i=1}^{\dot{s}}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}{\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}.

If
i\in I_{\dot{s}}^{*}
, it follows by (
5.41
) and (
5.32
) that

\begin{split}\Delta_{y_{i}}X=&-\nabla_{y_{i}}\cdot E_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*}),i}=\nabla_{y_{i}}\cdot\left(\frac{\mbox{$y_{i}-\sum_{\tau=i+1}^{i^{*}}y_{\tau}$}}{|\mbox{$y_{i}-\sum_{\tau=i+1}^{i^{*}}y_{\tau}$}|}-\frac{y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}}{|y_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}-\mbox{$\sum_{\tau=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)+1}^{i}y_{\tau}$}|}\right)\\
=&(n-1)\left(|r_{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i)}|^{-1}+|r_{i}|^{-1}\right),\end{split}

so we conclude by
I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2}=\cup_{i\in I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}\{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\dot{s},2}^{*},i),i\}
again that
\mathrm{IV}
is a finite linear combination of

\begin{split}\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-1}\tilde{V}\tilde{r}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+2)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}{\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}\\
=C\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}|r_{i_{0}}|^{-(n-2-l_{i_{0}}+\dot{d}_{i_{0}}+1)}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\dot{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{i_{0}\}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\dot{d}_{i})}\\
&\times\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+2)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}.\end{split}

(5.48)

where
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\dot{s}+1}}^{2}
.

The fifth type of integrals derived from (
5.42
) is

\mathrm{V}=\lambda^{-\dot{J}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\tilde{V}\tilde{r}\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|^{-(\dot{p}_{\dot{s}+1}+1)}\prod_{i<\dot{s}+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\dot{p}_{i}}}\left(\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}(\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi})\cdot\frac{\nabla_{y_{I_{\dot{s}}^{*}}}X}{\|F_{\dot{s}+1}\|}\right){\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k}.

By the properties presumed for
\dot{g}
and
\dot{\psi}
, with a mixture of the arguments for
\mathrm{II}
and
\mathrm{III}
, we conclude without more details that
\mathrm{V}
is a finite linear combination of the form (
5.37
) with properties that come along.

Now (
5.40
) when
i=\dot{s}+1
and the alternatives (
5.38
), (
5.39
) are obviously seen from (
5.44
), (
5.45
), (
5.46
), (
5.47
) ,(

), the discussion for
\mathrm{V}
, and (

), while the other cases in (

) is a result of (
5.36
) and the inclusions in (

).
∎

Now we are ready to show Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

The proof will be an induction on
\mu
by repeatedly using Lemma
5.9
. To satisfy the condition that
\dot{g}
being supported away from the origin in each of
r_{1},\cdots,r_{k},F_{1},\cdots,F_{\dot{s}+1}
, we in principle should first introduce cutoffs in
|r_{i}|/\epsilon
and
\|F_{i}\|/\epsilon
whose derivatives have the same type of bounds in the discussion for
\mathrm{II}
and
\mathrm{III}
in the proof of Lemma
5.9
, and let
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
when such application comes to an end. The convergence is actually a result of the fact that (
5.12
) is absolutely convergent for
\mu=k-k_{0}
, which is a consequence of Proposition
5.19
studied later. To avoid distraction, we will pretend that such condition on
\dot{g}
has been satisfied in the following application.

We first prove the statement for
\mu=1
. Note that Lemma
5.9
is first applicable to
U^{l}
, that is
\dot{s}=0
,
|\dot{\alpha}_{i}|\equiv 0
,
\dot{d}_{i}\equiv 0
,
\dot{p}_{1}=0
,
\dot{g}=f
,
\dot{\psi}=\phi
,
\dot{P}\equiv 1
,
I_{0,1}^{*}=\emptyset
,

I_{0,2}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};l_{i}+2-2m\leq 0\}=\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};0=\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\}\},

(5.49)

and therefore
I_{0}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K};l_{i}+2-2m>0\}\neq\emptyset
by the initial assumption (
5.5
). Inductively, after finitely many times of applying Lemma
5.9
whenever applicable to terms in the combination, we must end up with the fact that
U^{l}
is a finite linear combination of integrals in the form of

\begin{split}\lambda^{-\check{J}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\check{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\check{d}_{i})}\|F_{1}\|^{-{\check{p}_{1}}}\\
&\times\check{g}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k},F_{1})\check{\psi}(X/T){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.50)

satisfying

\begin{cases}\check{J}=|\check{\alpha}_{1}|+\cdots+|\check{\alpha}_{k}|+\check{d}_{0}+\cdots+\check{d}_{k},\\
|\check{\alpha}_{i}|=0,\quad&i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{F_{1}}^{1},\\
\check{d}_{i}=0,&i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{F_{1}}^{2},\\
\check{p}_{1}\leq\sum_{j\in I_{F_{1}}^{1}}|\check{\alpha}_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{1}}^{2}}2\check{d}_{j},\end{cases}

and the subordinate parameters are subject to three cases:

Case 1

There exists
i_{0}\in I_{F_{1}}^{1}
such that
|\check{\alpha}_{i_{0}}|=\frac{n+1}{2}-2m
and

\begin{cases}|\check{\alpha}_{i}|<\frac{n+1}{2}-2m,\quad&i\in I_{F_{1}}^{1}\setminus\{i_{0}\},\\
0\leq\check{d}_{i}<\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},&i\in I_{F_{1}}^{2},\\
L_{k_{0}}\leq\frac{n+1}{2}-2m\leq\check{J}\leq L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}.\end{cases}

In this case, we define
I_{1,1}^{*}=\{i_{0}\}
,
I_{1,2}^{*}=I_{0,2}^{*}
. It is obvious that

I_{1,1}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K};|\check{\alpha}_{i}|=\mbox{$\frac{n+2}{2}-2m$}\},

(5.51)

It also follows by (
5.22
) that

I_{F_{1}}^{2}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{0,2}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};l_{i}+2-2m>0\},

(5.52)

which combining with (
5.49
) implies

I_{1,2}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};\check{d}_{i}=\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\}\}.

(5.53)

Case 2

There exists
i_{0}\in I_{F_{1}}^{2}
such that
0<\check{d}_{i_{0}}=\max\{0,l_{i_{0}}+2-2m\}
and

\begin{cases}|\check{\alpha}_{i}|<\frac{n+1}{2}-2m,\quad&i\in I_{F_{1}}^{1},\\
0\leq\check{d}_{i}<\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},&i\in I_{F_{1}}^{2}\setminus\{i_{0}\},\\
L_{k_{0}}\leq\max\{0,l_{i_{0}}+2-2m\}\leq\check{J}\leq L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}.\end{cases}

In this case, we define
I_{1,1}^{*}=I_{0,1}^{*}=\emptyset
and
I_{1,2}^{*}=I_{0,2}^{*}\cup\{i_{0}\}
. Now (
5.52
) implies
i_{0}\notin I_{0,2}^{*}
and (
5.53
) holds in a similar way, while (
5.51
) holds trivially.

Case 3

It follows that

\begin{cases}|\check{\alpha}_{i}|<\frac{n+1}{2}-2m,\quad&i\in I_{F_{1}}^{1},\\
0\leq\check{d}_{i}<\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},&i\in I_{F_{1}}^{2},\\
\check{J}=L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}.\end{cases}

In this case, we just need to define
I_{1,1}^{*}=I_{0,1}^{*}
and
I_{1,2}^{*}=I_{0,2}^{*}
.

In all three cases, (
5.20
) holds for
i=1
by definition. Now all constraints are checked for
\mu=1
in the statement if we equip integral (
5.50
) with the sequences
I_{0,1}^{*}\subset I_{1,1}^{*}
and
I_{0,2}^{*}\subset I_{1,2}^{*}
defined respectively in the above three cases.

By induction, we now suppose
k-k_{0}\geq 2
and validity of the statement for some
\mu\in\{1,\cdots,k-k_{0}-1\}
. First note that for every integral (
5.12
) equipped with sequences (
5.13
) in the combination, if
J=L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}
, it is then trivial that all subordinate constraints remain true with
\mu
replaced by
\mu+1
.

So discussion is only needed when
s=\mu
and
J<L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}
, while we recall that
\tau^{(\mu)}
in (
5.15
) must exist. Now Lemma
5.9
is first applicable to (
5.12
), that is,
\dot{s}=\mu
,

\begin{cases}\dot{J}=J,\,\dot{p}_{\mu+1}=0,\\
\dot{\alpha}_{i}=\alpha_{i},\quad&i\in\mathbb{K},\\
\dot{d}_{i}=d_{i},&i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\\
\dot{p}_{i}=p_{i},&i=0,\cdots,\mu,\\
\end{cases}

sequences (
5.33
) given by (
5.13
),
\dot{g}=g
,
\dot{\psi}=\psi
and
\dot{P}=P
. This is because
I_{0}^{*},\cdots,I_{\mu}^{*}
are nonempty by Remark
5.2
, the inclusions in the first line of (
5.34
) are guaranteed by Lemma
5.5
, we also know by Lemma
5.7
that

\begin{cases}I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{1}\subset\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{\mu,1}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K};\,|\alpha_{i}|<\frac{n+1}{2}-2m\},\\
I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{2}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{\mu,2}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};\,0\leq d_{i}<\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\}\},\end{cases}

which checks the last two lines of (
5.34
), and (
5.36
) trivially holds. Inductively using Lemma
5.9
, we end up with the fact that integral (
5.12
) is a finite linear combination of integrals in the form of

\begin{split}\lambda^{-\tilde{J}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nk}}&e^{\mathrm{i}\lambda X}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}}V^{(\tilde{\alpha}_{i})}(x_{i})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}}|r_{i}|^{-(n-2-l_{i}+\tilde{d}_{i})}\prod_{i=1}^{\mu+1}\|F_{i}\|^{-{\tilde{p}_{i}}}\\
&\times\tilde{g}(\lambda,r_{0},\cdots,r_{k},F_{1},\cdots,F_{\mu+1})\tilde{\psi}(X/T){\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.54)

satisfying

\begin{cases}\tilde{J}=|\tilde{\alpha}_{1}|+\cdots+|\tilde{\alpha}_{k}|+\tilde{d}_{0}+\cdots+\tilde{d}_{k},\\
\tilde{\alpha}_{i}=\alpha_{i},\quad&i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{1},\\
\tilde{d}_{i}=d_{i},&i\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{2},\\
\tilde{p}_{i}+\cdots+\tilde{p}_{s+1}\leq\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\tilde{\alpha}_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}2\tilde{d}_{j},&i=1,\cdots,\mu+1,\end{cases}

(5.55)

and the subordinate parameters are subject to three cases:

Case 1’

There exists
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{1}
such that
|\tilde{\alpha}_{i_{0}}|=\frac{n+1}{2}-2m
and

\begin{cases}|\tilde{\alpha}_{i}|<\frac{n+1}{2}-2m,\quad&i\in I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{1}\setminus\{i_{0}\},\\
0\leq\tilde{d}_{i}<\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},&i\in I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{2},\\
L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k_{0}+\mu}\leq\tilde{J}\leq L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}.\end{cases}

In this case, we define
I_{\mu+1,1}^{*}=I_{\mu,1}^{*}\cup\{i_{0}\}
and
I_{\mu+1,2}^{*}=I_{\mu,2}^{*}
.

Case 2’

There exists
i_{0}\in I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{2}
such that
0<\tilde{d}_{i_{0}}=\max\{0,l_{i_{0}}+2-2m\}

\begin{cases}|\tilde{\alpha}_{i}|<\frac{n+1}{2}-2m,\quad&i\in I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{1},\\
0\leq\tilde{d}_{i}<\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},&i\in I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{2}\setminus\{i_{0}\},\\
L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k_{0}+\mu}\leq\tilde{J}\leq L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}.\end{cases}

In this case, we define
I_{\mu+1,1}^{*}=I_{\mu,1}^{*}
and
I_{\mu+1,2}^{*}=I_{\mu,2}^{*}\cup\{i_{0}\}
.

Case 3’

It follows that

\begin{cases}|\tilde{\alpha}_{i}|<\frac{n+1}{2}-2m,\quad&i\in I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{1},\\
0\leq\tilde{d}_{i}<\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\},&i\in I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{2},\\
\tilde{J}=L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k-1}.\end{cases}

In this case, we define
I_{\mu+1,1}^{*}=I_{\mu,1}^{*}
and
I_{\mu+1,2}^{*}=I_{\mu,2}^{*}
.

The remaining discussion is parallel to those from Case 1 to Case 3 previously. That
i_{0}\notin I_{\mu,1}^{*}
in Case 1’ and that
i_{0}\notin I_{\mu,2}^{*}
in Case 2’ are implied by Lemma
5.7
. We also conclude in all three cases that (
5.20
) holds for
i=\mu+1
by definition, and that

\begin{cases}I_{\mu+1,1}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K};\,|\tilde{\alpha}_{i}|=\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}\},\\
I_{\mu+1,2}^{*}=\{i\in\mathbb{K}_{0};\,\tilde{d}_{i}=\max\{0,l_{i}+2-2m\}\},\end{cases}

which follows by (
5.55
), the definitions of
I_{\mu,1}^{*}
and
I_{\mu,2}^{*}
, and the consequence of Lemma
5.7
saying

I_{\mu,1}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{1},\quad I_{\mu,2}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{F_{\mu+1}}^{2}.

Now integral (
5.54
) satisfies all constraints in the statement for
\mu+1
if we equip it with the sequences
I_{0,1}^{*}\subset\cdots\subset I_{\mu,1}^{*}\subset I_{\mu+1,1}^{*}
and
I_{0,2}^{*}\subset\cdots\subset I_{\mu,2}^{*}\subset I_{\mu+1,2}^{*}
defined respectively above, and the proof is complete.
∎

5.3.
Reduction of line singularities

We now turn to a reduction of the family of
\|F_{i}\|
in the integral (
5.12
), and the main result in this part is Proposition
5.14
. The key property of
F_{1},\cdots,F_{s}
we observe is that the subscripts of their elements have a nested pattern described by the following.

Definition 5.10
.

We call subset
A
of
\mathbb{N}_{0}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}
is admissible, if

1)

(i,j)\in A
implies
i<j
.

2)

(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2})\in A
and
i_{1}<j_{2}<j_{1}
imply
i_{1}\leq i_{2}
.

If integral (
5.12
) in Proposition
5.1
is given with
s\geq 2
, then
\tau^{(1)},\cdots,\tau^{(s-1)}
defined in (
5.15
) must exist, and it has been shown in (
5.25
) that

I_{i-1}^{*}\setminus I_{i}^{*}=\{j^{(i)}\}=\{N(I_{i-1}^{*},\tau^{(i)})\},\quad i=1,\cdots,s-1,

We define for
j\in\mathbb{K}
that

\begin{split}\iota_{j}&=\begin{cases}j-1,&j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{0}^{*},\\
L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j),\quad&j\in I_{i-1}^{*}\setminus I_{i}^{*},\,i=1,\cdots,s-1,\\
L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*},j),&j\in I_{s-1}^{*},\end{cases}\\
&=\begin{cases}j-1,&j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{0}^{*},\\
L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j^{(i)}),\quad&j=j^{(i)},\,i=1,\cdots,s-1,\\
L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*},j),&j\in I_{s-1}^{*}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(5.56)

It immediately follows that
\iota_{j}<j
and

\begin{split}E_{\iota_{j^{(i)}},j^{(i)}}\in F_{i},\quad&i=1,\cdots,s-1,\\
E_{\iota_{j},j}\in F_{s},\quad&j\in I_{s-1}^{*}.\end{split}

(5.57)

We note in advance that the following property is crucial for Proposition
5.19
in the next section.

Proposition 5.11
.

If integral (
5.12
) in Proposition
5.1
is given with
s\geq 2
, then
\{(\iota_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
is admissible.

Proof.

Suppose
\iota_{j}<j^{\prime}<j
for some
j\in\mathbb{K}
. We first note that
j\in I_{0}^{*}
must hold because
\iota_{j}\leq j-2
, and the conclusion is obviously trivial when
j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{0}^{*}
.

If
j^{\prime}\in I_{0}^{*}
and
\iota_{j}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j)
for some
i=1,\cdots,s
, we know by
\iota_{j}<j^{\prime}<j
that
j^{\prime}\notin\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}
. Since
I_{i-1}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}
, then
j^{\prime}\notin I_{i-1}^{*}
holds and thus
j^{\prime}\in I_{0}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1}^{*}
follows. Therefore, there exists
i^{\prime}\in\mathbb{K}
with
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i-1
such that
I_{i^{\prime}-1}\setminus I_{i^{\prime}}^{*}=\{j^{\prime}\}
, and consequently

\iota_{j^{\prime}}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i^{\prime}-1,2}^{*},j^{\prime})\geq L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j^{\prime})=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*},j)=\iota_{j},

where the inequality is a result of
I_{i^{\prime}-1,2}^{*}\subset I_{i-1,2}^{*}
, and the second equality is a conclusion of
\iota_{j}<j^{\prime}<j
again.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

Suppose integral (
5.12
) in Proposition
5.1
is given with
s\geq 2
, and

p_{i}+\cdots+p_{s}\leq\tilde{p}_{i}+\cdots+\tilde{p}_{s},\quad i=1,\cdots,s,

(5.58)

where
\tilde{p}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{p}_{s}\geq 0
. Then

\|F_{1}\|^{-p_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}\lesssim\|F_{1}\|^{-\tilde{p}_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-\tilde{p}_{s}}.

Proof.

Lemma
5.5
implies that
1\gtrsim\|F_{1}\|\geq\cdots\geq\|F_{s}\|
, so

\begin{split}\|F_{1}\|^{-p_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}=&\|F_{1}\|^{-\tilde{p}_{1}}\|F_{1}\|^{-(p_{1}-\tilde{p}_{1})}\|F_{2}\|^{-p_{2}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}\\
\lesssim&\|F_{1}\|^{-\tilde{p}_{1}}\times\begin{cases}\|F_{2}\|^{-p_{2}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}},\quad&\text{if}\,p_{1}\leq\tilde{p}_{1},\\
\|F_{2}\|^{-(p_{1}+p_{2}-\tilde{p}_{1})}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}},&\text{if}\,p_{1}>\tilde{p}_{1},\end{cases}\end{split}

and the conclusion follows by an induction on
s
if we use (
5.58
).
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

If integral (
5.12
) in Proposition
5.1
is given with
s\geq 2
, it follows that

\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}d_{j}\leq J-(L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k_{0}+i-2}),\quad i=2,\cdots,s.

(5.59)

Proof.

The inclusions (
5.13
) imply

|\alpha_{j}|=\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}\geq L_{\tau},\quad j\in I_{i-1,1}^{*},

for any
k_{0}\leq\tau\leq k
because
l_{\tau}\leq\frac{n-3}{2}
, and also imply

d_{j}=L_{\sigma^{-1}(j)}>0,\quad j\in I_{i-1,2}^{*}\setminus I_{0,2}^{*},

because
d_{j}=\max\{0,l_{j}+2-2m\}=l_{j}+2-2m>0
must hold. Since the constraint 2) in Proposition
5.1
also implies
\#I_{i-1,1}^{*}+\#(I_{i-1,2}^{*}\setminus I_{0,2}^{*})=i-1
, so

\begin{split}J\geq&\left(\sum_{j\in I_{i-1,1}^{*}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{i-1,2}^{*}\setminus I_{0,2}^{*}}d_{j}\right)+\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{i-1,1}^{*}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}}d_{j}\right)\\
\geq&\left(L_{k_{0}}+\cdots+L_{k_{0}+i-2}\right)+\left(\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}d_{j}\right),\end{split}

and the last line is a consequence of Lemma
5.7
.
∎

Proposition 5.14
.

Suppose integral (
5.12
) in Proposition
5.1
is given, and
\{E_{\iota_{j},j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{K}}
is defined through (
5.56
).

1)

If
s\geq 2
and
I_{s-1,1}^{*}\neq\emptyset
, which guarantees the existence of
i_{0}\in\{1,\cdots,s-1\}
with
I_{i_{0},1}^{*}\setminus I_{i_{0}-1,1}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i_{0})}\}
and
I_{i_{0},2}^{*}=I_{i_{0}-1,2}^{*}
, then

\|F_{1}\|^{-p_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}\lesssim|E_{\iota_{j^{(i_{0})}},j^{(i_{0})}}|^{-(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}\prod_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{j^{(i_{0})}\}}|E_{\iota_{j},j}|^{-(n+1-4m)}.

(5.60)

If
j^{(i_{0})}<k
, then
j^{(i_{0})}\leq\iota_{j}
holds for all
j\in\{j^{(i_{0})}+1,\cdots,k\}
.

2)

If
s\geq 2
and
I_{s-1,1}^{*}=\emptyset
, then

\|F_{1}\|^{-p_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}\lesssim|E_{\iota_{k},k}|^{-\min\{n+1-4m,\frac{n+1}{2}-2m+d_{0}+d_{k}\}}\prod_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{k\}}|E_{\iota_{j},j}|^{-(n+1-4m)},

which also holds if
s=1
.

Proof.

First note that (
5.57
) implies

\begin{split}\|F_{i}\|\geq|E_{\iota_{j^{(i)}},j^{(i)}}|,\quad&i=1,\cdots,s-1,\\
\|F_{s}\|\geq|E_{\iota_{j},j}|,\quad&j\in I_{s-1}^{*}.\end{split}

(5.61)

We first show 1). If
1\leq i<i_{0}
, since
I_{i_{0},1}^{*}\setminus I_{i_{0}-1,1}^{*}=\{\tau^{(i_{0})}\}
implies
\tau^{(i_{0})}\in I_{F_{i_{0}}}^{1}\subset I_{F_{i}}^{1}
by (
5.20
) and Lemma
5.5
, we deduce

\begin{split}p_{i}+\cdots+p_{s}=&2\left(\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}d_{j}\right)-\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|\\
\leq&\begin{cases}2J-|\alpha_{i_{0}}|,\quad&i=1,\\
2(J-L_{k_{0}}-\cdots-L_{k_{0}+i-2})-|\alpha_{i_{0}}|,&1<i<i_{0},\end{cases}\\
\leq&2(L_{k_{0}+i-1}+\cdots+L_{k-1})-(\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$})\\
\leq&(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-i)+(\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}),\end{split}

where we have used (
5.18
), (
5.59
), the last inequality in (
5.14
), and the fact that
|\alpha_{i_{0}}|=\frac{n+1}{2}-2m
for
\tau^{(i_{0})}\in I_{i_{0},1}^{*}\subset I_{s,1}^{*}
. If
i_{0}\leq i\leq s
, we always have by (
5.59
) that

\begin{split}p_{i}+\cdots+p_{s}\leq&2\left(\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}d_{j}\right)\\
\leq&\begin{cases}2J,\quad&i=1,\\
2(J-L_{k_{0}}-\cdots-L_{k_{0}+i-2}),&1<i\leq s,\end{cases}\\
\leq&2(L_{k_{0}+i-1}+\cdots+L_{k-1})\\
\leq&(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-i+1).\end{split}

(5.62)

Applying Lemma
5.12
with

\tilde{p}_{i}=\begin{cases}n+1-4m,\quad&i\notin\{i_{0},s\},\\
\frac{n+1}{2}-2m,&i=i_{0},\\
(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-s+1),&i=s,\end{cases}

and we get by using (
5.61
),
I_{0}^{*}\setminus I_{s-1}^{*}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s-1}\{j^{(i)}\}
and
\#I_{s-1}^{*}=k-k_{0}-s+1
that

\begin{split}\|F_{1}\|^{-p_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}\lesssim&\|F_{i_{0}}\|^{-(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq s-1\\
i\neq i_{0}\end{subarray}}\|F_{i}\|^{-(n+1-4m)}\right)\|F_{s}\|^{-(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-s+1)}\\
\leq&|E_{\iota_{j^{(i_{0})}},j^{(i_{0})}}|^{-(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq s-1\\
i\neq i_{0}\end{subarray}}|E_{\iota_{j^{(i)}},j^{(i)}}|^{-(n+1-4m)}\prod_{j\in I_{s-1}^{*}}|E_{\iota_{j},j}|^{-(n+1-4m)}\\
=&|E_{\iota_{j^{(i_{0})}},j^{(i_{0})}}|^{-(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}\prod_{j\in I_{0}^{*}\setminus\{j^{(i_{0})}\}}|E_{\iota_{j},j}|^{-(n+1-4m)},\end{split}

(5.63)

which implies (
5.60
) for
|E_{\iota_{j},j}|\lesssim 1
. Now suppose
j^{(i_{0})}<k
and

\iota_{i}<j^{(i_{0})}<i,

(5.64)

for some
i\in\{j^{(i_{0})}+1,\cdots,k\}
, which indicates
\iota_{i}\leq i-2
, thus

i\in I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1}^{*},

(5.65)

and
\iota_{i}=L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1,2}^{*},i)
hold for some
i_{0}^{\prime}\geq 1
by (
5.56
). Note that
i_{0}^{\prime}>i_{0}
must hold, because otherwise
i_{0}^{\prime}<i_{0}
for
j^{(i_{0})}\neq i
, and then
j^{(i_{0})}\in I_{i_{0}-1}^{*}\subset I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1,2}^{*}
yields the contradiction
\iota_{i}\geq j^{(i_{0})}
to (
5.64
). On the other hand, we have shown at the beginning of the proof that
\tau^{(i_{0})}\in I_{F_{i_{0}}}^{1}
, so there in the view of (
5.22
) exists some
j\in I_{i_{0}-1}^{*}
with

L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i_{0}-1,2}^{*},j)<\tau^{(i_{0})}\leq j.

(5.66)

Note that
j\in I_{i_{0}-1}^{*}
implies
N(I_{i_{0}-1}^{*},\tau^{(i_{0})})\leq j
, that is
j^{(i_{0})}\leq j
. It is in fact impossible for
j^{(i_{0})}<j
to hold, otherwise, the fact by definition that
j^{(i_{0})}\in I_{i_{0}-1}^{*}\subset\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i_{0}-1,2}^{*}
implies
j^{(i_{0})}\leq L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i_{0}-1,2}^{*},j)<j
, and the contradiction
\tau^{(i_{0})}\leq N(I_{i_{0}-1}^{*},\tau^{(i_{0})})=j^{(i_{0})}\leq L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i_{0}-1,2}^{*},j)<\tau^{(i_{0})}
to (
5.66
). Now we must have
j=j^{(i_{0})}
, that is

L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i_{0}-1,2}^{*},j^{(i_{0})})<\tau^{(i_{0})}\leq j^{(i_{0})}.

This, and the fact
\{j^{(i_{0})},\cdots,i-1\}\subset I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1,2}^{*}
implied by (
5.64
), together show
\{\tau^{(i_{0})},\cdots,i-1\}\subset I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1,2}^{*}
, and consequently

I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1}^{*}=D_{I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1,1}^{*}}D_{I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1,2}^{*}}\mathbb{K}\subset D_{\tau^{(i_{0})}}D_{\{\tau^{(i_{0})},\cdots,i-1\}}\mathbb{K},

which indicates the contradiction
i\notin I_{i_{0}^{\prime}-1}^{*}
to (
5.65
). Therefore, (
5.64
) is not true, and the proof of 1) is complete.

To show 2), if
s\geq 2
,
I_{s-1,1}^{*}=\emptyset
and
\{0,k\}\cap I_{s-1,2}^{*}\neq\emptyset
, note that (
5.62
) is true for
i=1,\cdots,s
, we may apply Lemma
5.12
with

\tilde{p}_{i}=\begin{cases}n+1-4m,\quad&i=1,\cdots,s-1,\\
(k-k_{0}-s+1)(n+1-4m),&i=s,\end{cases}

and similar to (
5.63
), we deduce

\begin{split}\|F_{1}\|^{-p_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}\lesssim&\left(\prod_{1\leq i\leq s-1}\|F_{i}\|^{-(n+1-4m)}\right)\|F_{s}\|^{-(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-s+1)}\\
\leq&\prod_{1\leq i\leq s-1}|E_{\iota_{j^{(i)}},j^{(i)}}|^{-(n+1-4m)}\prod_{j\in I_{s-1}^{*}}|E_{\iota_{j},j}|^{-(n+1-4m)}\\
\leq&\prod_{j\in\mathbb{K}}|E_{\iota_{j},j}|^{-(n+1-4m)},\end{split}

and this is a stronger estimate for
n+1-4m\leq\frac{n+1}{2}-2m+d_{0}+d_{k}
holds when
\{0,k\}\cap I_{s-1,2}^{*}\neq\emptyset
.

If
I_{s-1,1}^{*}=\emptyset
and
\{0,k\}\cap I_{s-1,2}^{*}=\emptyset
, then

I_{s-1}^{*}=D_{I_{s-1,2}^{*}}\mathbb{K}=\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}\ni k,

(5.67)

so it is easy to get from (
5.22
) that

\begin{split}I_{F_{s}}^{1}=&\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}\{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*},j)+1,\cdots,j\}=\mathbb{K},\\
I_{F_{s}}^{2}=&\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}\{L(\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*},j),j\}=\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}.\end{split}

(5.68)

Next for
i=1,\cdots,s
,

\begin{split}p_{i}+\cdots+p_{s}\leq&\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{i}}^{2}}2d_{j}\\
\leq&\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}}2d_{j}\\
=&\sum_{j\in I_{F_{s}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}2d_{j}+\sum_{j\in I_{s-1,2}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*}}2d_{j}\\
\leq&\sum_{j\in I_{F_{s}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}2d_{j}+(n+1-4m)(s-i),\end{split}

(5.69)

where we have used Lemma
5.7
and the fact that
\#(I_{s-1,2}^{*}\setminus I_{i-1,2}^{*})\leq s-i
. Further,

\begin{split}&\sum_{j\in I_{F_{s}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}2d_{j}=\sum_{j\in I_{F_{s}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{s}}^{2}}d_{j}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}d_{j}\\
\leq&L_{k_{0}+s-1}+\cdots+L_{k-1}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}d_{j}\\
\leq&(\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$})(k-k_{0}-s+1)+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}d_{j}\\
=&(\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$})(k-k_{0}-s)+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus(I_{s-1,2}^{*}\cup\{k\})}d_{j}+(\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$})+d_{0}+d_{k}\\
\leq&(\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$})(k-k_{0}-s)+(\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$})\#\left(\mathbb{K}\setminus(I_{s-1,2}^{*}\cup\{k\})\right)+\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}+d_{0}+d_{k}\\
\leq&(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-s)+\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}+d_{0}+d_{k},\end{split}

(5.70)

where we have used (
5.68
), an application of (
5.59
) that is similar to (
5.62
), the assumption
\{0,k\}\cap I_{s-1,2}^{*}=\emptyset
, and the fact that
\#(\mathbb{K}\setminus(I_{s-1,2}^{*}\cup\{k\}))=k-k_{0}-s
which is due to
\emptyset=I_{s-1,1}^{*}=\cdots=I_{0,1}^{*}
and consequently
I_{s-1,2}^{*}=I_{0,2}^{*}\cup\{\tau^{(1)},\cdots,\tau^{(s-1)}\}
. On the other hand, we also have

\begin{split}\sum_{j\in I_{F_{s}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus I_{s-1,2}^{*}}2d_{j}\leq&2\left(\sum_{j\in I_{F_{s}}^{1}}|\alpha_{j}|+\sum_{j\in I_{F_{s}}^{2}}d_{j}\right)\\
\leq&2(L_{k_{0}+s-1}+\cdots+L_{k-1})\\
\leq&(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-s+1).\end{split}

(5.71)

Combining (
5.70
), (
5.71
) and (
5.69
), we derive for
i=i,\cdots,s
that

\begin{split}&p_{i}+\cdots+p_{s}\\
\leq&(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-i)+\min(n+1-4m,\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}+d_{0}+d_{k}),\end{split}

so we may apply Lemma
5.12
with

\tilde{p}_{i}=\begin{cases}n+1-4m,\quad&i=1,\cdots,s-1,\\
(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-s)+\min\{n+1-4m,\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}-2m$}+d_{0}+d_{k}\},&i=s,\end{cases}

to deduce

\begin{split}&\|F_{1}\|^{-p_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}\\
\lesssim&\left(\prod_{1\leq i\leq s-1}\|F_{i}\|^{-(n+1-4m)}\right)\|F_{s}\|^{-(n+1-4m)(k-k_{0}-s)-\min\{n+1-4m,\frac{n+1}{2}-2m+d_{0}+d_{k}\}}\\
\leq&|E_{\iota_{k},k}|^{-\min\{n+1-4m,\frac{n+1}{2}-2m+d_{0}+d_{k}\}}\prod_{1\leq i\leq s-1}|E_{\iota_{j^{(i)}},j^{(i)}}|^{-(n+1-4m)}\prod_{j\in I_{s-1}^{*}\setminus\{k\}}|E_{\iota_{j},j}|^{-(n+1-4m)}\\
\leq&|E_{\iota_{k},k}|^{-\min\{n+1-4m,\frac{n+1}{2}-2m+d_{0}+d_{k}\}}\prod_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{k\}}|E_{\iota_{j},j}|^{-(n+1-4m)},\end{split}

where in the second inequality we have used (
5.67
) and consequently
\#(I_{s-1}^{*}\setminus\{k\})=k-k_{0}-s
. Now the proof of 2) is complete.
∎

5.4.
Estimates for integrals with point and reduced line singularities

Since
\|F_{1}\|^{-p_{1}}\cdots\|F_{s}\|^{-p_{s}}
in (
5.12
) has been estimated in Proposition
5.14
in particular forms, we now need to consider the estimates for integrals in the form of

\begin{split}&I_{k}(x_{0},x_{k+1};\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{k},a_{0},\cdots,a_{k},q_{1},\cdots,q_{k})\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}\cdots\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}}{|x_{0}-x_{1}|^{a_{0}}\cdots|x_{k}-x_{k+1}|^{a_{k}}}|E_{\eta_{1},1}|^{-q_{1}}\cdots|E_{\eta_{k},k}|^{-q_{k}}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k},\end{split}

(5.72)

based on the three lemmas (Lemma
5.16
to Lemma
5.18
), which together prove the second main technical result Proposition
5.19
in Section
5
.

Before introducing these results, it is crucial to note that when there are a lot of line singularities
|E_{\eta_{j},j}|^{-q_{j}}
in the integral (
5.72
), the possibility of bounding such integrals will come from further assuming
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
to be admissible by Definition
5.10
, because then we are always able to choose a specific variable
x_{\tau}
such that Proposition
2.4
is first applicable in the integral of
x_{\tau}
where at most two line singularities are relevant, and the admissibility of
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
will also allow such mechanism after each time of applying Proposition
2.4
. The choice of such
x_{\tau}
is asked to obey certain constraints for technical reasons.

Proposition 5.15
.

Suppose that
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
in (
5.72
) is admissible. Then we can find
\tau\in\mathbb{K}
such that
x_{\tau}
is independent of
E_{\eta_{j},j}
unless
j\in\{\tau-1,\tau\}
, and we also assert the following:

(i) If there exists
s\in\mathbb{K}
with
\eta_{s}\leq s-3
, then
\tau
can be chosen to satisfy
\eta_{s}<\tau<s
.

(ii)
\tau
can always be chosen to satisfy either

\eta_{k}\leq\eta_{\tau+1}<\eta_{\tau}<\tau<\tau+1\leq k,

(5.73)

or

\eta_{k}<\tau=k.

(5.74)

Proof.

To prove (i), let

\tau=\max\{j\in\{1,\cdots,s-1\};~\eta_{j}=j-1~\text{or}~\eta_{j}=\eta_{j-1}\},

(5.75)

which is well defined because
\eta_{1}=0
. If
\tau<s-1
, from the admissibility of
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
and that
\tau
being the greatest, we must have

\eta_{\tau+1}<\eta_{\tau}<\tau<\tau+1,

(5.76)

thus for
j=\tau+1,\cdots,k
, it follows from the admissibility again that either
\eta_{j}\geq\tau+1
or
\eta_{j}\leq\eta_{\tau+1}
holds, and consequently

\eta_{j}\notin\{\tau-1,\tau\},\quad j=\tau+1,\cdots,k.

(5.77)

If
\tau=s-1
, then (
5.77
) is also true, because the admissibility implies either
\eta_{j}\geq s
or
\eta_{j}\leq\eta_{s}
, and recall that we have assumed
\eta_{s}\leq s-3=\tau-2
. It is now clear that

\eta_{s}<\tau<s,

(5.78)

and
E_{\eta_{j},j}
depends on
x_{\tau}
only when
j\in\{\tau-1,\tau\}
.

To prove (ii), if
\eta_{k}\geq k-2
, we can just choose
\tau=k
because
\eta_{j}<k-2
for each
j<k-2
. If
\eta_{k}\leq k-3
, we may use the first assertion with
s=k
to choose
\tau
, and then (
5.76
) implies either (
5.73
) or (
5.74
).
∎

We now turn back to the estimates of (
5.72
) in different regimes of assumption on indices.

Lemma 5.16
.

Suppose
n\geq 4m+1
,
k\geq 2
,
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
is admissible in (
5.72
), and

\begin{cases}n-2m\leq a_{i}\leq n-2\,(1\leq i\leq k-1),\,\,0\leq a_{0},a_{k}\leq n-2,\,\,a_{0}+a_{k}\geq\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}$},\\
\beta_{i}\geq 2m\,(i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{k\}),\,\,\beta_{k}\geq\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}$},\\
q_{i}=n+1-4m\,(1\leq i\leq k-1),\,\,0\leq q_{k}\leq\min\{a_{0}+a_{k},n+1-4m\}.\end{cases}

(5.79)

Then

I_{k}(x_{0},x_{k+1};\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{k},a_{0},\cdots,a_{k},q_{1},\cdots,q_{k})\lesssim 1,\quad|x_{0}-x_{k+1}|\gtrsim 1.

Proof.

If
k=2
, then
\{E_{\eta_{1},1},E_{\eta_{2}},2\}=\{E_{0,1},E_{\eta_{2},2}\}
, and one checks with Proposition
2.4
in both situations that

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-(\beta_{1}+\epsilon)}|r_{0}|^{-a_{0}}|r_{1}|^{-a_{1}}|E_{0,1}|^{-q_{1}}|E_{\eta_{2},2}|^{-q_{2}}{\rm d}x_{1}\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}{\rm d}x_{1}}{|x_{0}-x_{1}|^{a_{0}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{a_{1}}|E_{x_{0}x_{1}x_{1}x_{2}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{x_{\eta_{2}}x_{\eta_{2}+1}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{q_{2}}},\\
\lesssim&|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{-q_{2}}|x_{0}-x_{2}|^{-\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}},\quad|x_{0}-x_{2}|>0,\end{split}

and therefore

I_{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-\beta_{2}-}{\rm d}x_{2}}{|x_{0}-x_{2}|^{\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{a_{2}}|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{q_{2}}}\lesssim 1,\quad|x_{0}-x_{3}|\gtrsim 1,

where we have used the facts that
\beta_{2}\geq\frac{n+1}{2}
,

\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}+a_{2}+\beta_{2}-n\geq\min\{a_{0}+a_{2},n-2m\}-\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}$}\geq 0,

and

\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}+a_{2}-q_{2}\geq\min\{a_{0}+a_{2},n-2m\}-\min\{a_{0}+a_{2},n+1-4m\}\geq 0.

If
k\geq 3
, we split the proof into three cases.

Case 1: There exists
s\in\{3,\cdots,k\}
with
\eta_{s}\leq s-3
.

By the first assertion of Proposition
5.15
, we can find
\tau\in\mathbb{K}
with
\eta_{s}<\tau<s
, so that
E_{\eta_{j},j}
depends on
x_{\tau}
only when
j\in\{\tau-1,\tau\}
, and then the integral with respect to
x_{\tau}
reads

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{\tau}\rangle^{-\beta_{\tau}-}{\rm d}x_{\tau}}{|x_{\tau-1}-x_{\tau}|^{a_{\tau-1}}|x_{\tau}-x_{\tau+1}|^{a_{\tau}}|E_{\eta_{\tau-1},\tau-1}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{\eta_{\tau},\tau}|^{n+1-4m}}.

(5.80)

If
\eta_{\tau}=\eta_{\tau-1}
, the triangle inequality implies

\begin{split}&|E_{\eta_{\tau-1},\tau-1}|^{-(n+1-4m)}|E_{\eta_{\tau},\tau}|^{-(n+1-4m)}\\
\lesssim&|E_{\eta_{\tau},\tau}|^{-(n+1-4m)}|E_{\tau-1,\tau}|^{-(n+1-4m)}+|E_{\eta_{\tau-1},\tau-1}|^{-(n+1-4m)}|E_{\tau-1,\tau}|^{-(n+1-4m)},\end{split}

(5.81)

we may thus just consider
\eta_{\tau}=\eta_{\tau-1}
in the view of (
5.75
), and apply Proposition
2.4
to (
5.80
) and (
5.81
), to conclude that integral (
5.80
) is bounded by

|E_{x_{\eta_{\tau-1}}x_{\eta_{\tau-1}+1}x_{\tau-1}x_{\tau+1}}|^{-(n+1-4m)}|x_{\tau-1}-x_{\tau+1}|^{-\min\{a_{\tau-1},a_{\tau}\}},

where we have used
\beta_{\tau}\geq 2m
which always holds. Consequently,

\begin{split}I_{k}\lesssim&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)n}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}\cdots\langle x_{\tau-1}\rangle^{-\beta_{\tau-1}-}\langle x_{\tau+1}\rangle^{-\beta_{\tau+1}-}\cdots\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}}{|r_{0}|^{a_{0}}\cdots|r_{\tau-2}|^{a_{\tau-2}}|x_{\tau-1}-x_{\tau+1}|^{\min\{a_{\tau-1},a_{\tau}\}}|r_{\tau+1}|^{a_{\tau+1}}\cdots|r_{k}|^{a_{k}}}\\
&\quad\quad\times\frac{{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{\tau-1}{\rm d}x_{\tau}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}}{|E_{x_{\eta_{\tau-1}}x_{\eta_{\tau-1}+1}x_{\tau-1}x_{\tau+1}}|^{n+1-4m}\prod\limits_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{\tau-1,\tau\}}|E_{x_{\eta_{j}}x_{\eta_{j}+1}x_{j}x_{j+1}}|^{q_{j}}}.\end{split}

(5.82)

Now we relabel

y_{j}=\begin{cases}x_{j},\quad&0\leq j\leq\tau-1,\\
x_{j+1}&\tau\leq j\leq k,\end{cases}\quad\tilde{\beta}_{j}=\begin{cases}\beta_{j},\quad&1\leq j\leq\tau-1,\\
\beta_{j+1}&\tau\leq j\leq k-1,\end{cases}

and

\tilde{a}_{j}=\begin{cases}a_{j},\quad&0\leq j\leq\tau-2,\\
\min(a_{\tau-1},a_{\tau}),&j=\tau-1,\\
a_{j+1}&\tau\leq j\leq k-1,\end{cases}\quad\tilde{q}_{j}=\begin{cases}q_{j},\quad&\text{if}~\tau\geq 3,~1\leq j\leq\tau-2,\\
n+1-4m,&j=\tau-1,\\
q_{j+1},&\tau\leq j\leq k-1.\end{cases}

Note that
\eta_{\tau-1}<\tau-1
implies
E_{x_{\eta_{\tau-1}}x_{\eta_{\tau-1}+1}x_{\tau-1}x_{\tau+1}}=E_{y_{\eta_{\tau-1}}y_{\eta_{\tau-1}+1}y_{\tau-1}y_{\tau}}
. If
1\leq j\leq\tau-2
, it is also obvious from the admissibility that
E_{x_{\eta_{j}}x_{\eta_{j}+1}x_{j}x_{j+1}}=E_{y_{\eta_{j}}y_{\eta_{j}+1}y_{j}y_{j+1}}
. If
\tau+1\leq j\leq k
, the argument from (
5.76
) to (
5.78
) also shows that

E_{x_{\eta_{j}}x_{\eta_{j}+1}x_{j}x_{j+1}}=\begin{cases}E_{y_{\eta_{j}}y_{\eta_{j}+1}y_{j-1}y_{j}},\quad&\text{if}~\eta_{j}\leq\tau-2,\\
E_{y_{\eta_{j}-1}y_{\eta_{j}}y_{j-1}y_{j}},&\text{if}~\eta_{j}\geq\tau+1.\end{cases}

In other words, if we denote

\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\begin{cases}\eta_{j},\quad&1\leq j\leq\tau-1,\\
\eta_{j+1},&\text{if}~\tau\leq j\leq k-1~\text{and}~\eta_{j+1}\leq\tau-2,\\
\eta_{j+1}-1,&\text{if}~\tau\leq j\leq k-1~\text{and}~\eta_{j+1}\geq\tau+1,\end{cases}

then (
5.82
) says

I_{k}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)n}}\frac{\langle y_{1}\rangle^{-\tilde{\beta}_{1}-}\cdots\langle y_{k-1}\rangle^{-\tilde{\beta}_{k-1}-}}{|y_{0}-y_{1}|^{\tilde{a}_{0}}\cdots|y_{k-1}-y_{k}|^{\tilde{a}_{k-1}}}|E_{\tilde{\eta}_{1},1}|^{-\tilde{q}_{1}}\cdots|E_{\tilde{\eta}_{k-1},k-1}|^{-\tilde{q}_{k-1}}{\rm d}y_{1}\cdots{\rm d}y_{k-1}.

(5.83)

We claim that
\{(\tilde{\eta}_{j},j);~j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{k\}\}
is admissible. In fact, that
\tilde{\eta}_{j}<j
is obvious by definition, and to check condition 2) in Definition
5.10
, it is only necessary to discuss when
\tau\leq j\leq k-1
and
\tilde{\eta}_{j}<i<j
, where there are four possibilities:

1)

If
i\leq\tau-1
, we must know
\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\eta_{j+1}
, so
\tilde{\eta}_{j}\leq\eta_{i}=\tilde{\eta}_{i}
is obvious.

2)

If
i\geq\tau
and
\tilde{\eta}_{i}=\eta_{i+1}
, it is easy to see from
\tilde{\eta}_{j}<i<j
that
\eta_{j+1}<i+1<j+1
, and therefore
\tilde{\eta}_{j}\leq\eta_{j+1}\leq\eta_{i+1}=\tilde{\eta}_{i}
.

3)

If
i\geq\tau
,
\tilde{\eta}_{i}=\eta_{i+1}-1
and
\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\eta_{j+1}-1
, then
\eta_{j+1}<i+1<j+1
and thus
\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\eta_{j+1}-1\leq\eta_{i+1}-1=\tilde{\eta}_{i}
.

4)

If
i\geq\tau
,
\tilde{\eta}_{i}=\eta_{i+1}-1
and
\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\eta_{j+1}
, then
\eta_{j+1}\leq\tau-2
,
\eta_{i+1}\geq\tau+1
, and thus
\tilde{\eta}_{j}\leq\tilde{\eta}_{i}-2
.

Now it is routine to check the relevant conditions in (
5.79
) with respect to
\tilde{a}_{i}
,
\tilde{\beta}_{i}
and
\tilde{q}_{i}
for the RHS of (
5.83
). Thus the estimate of
I_{k}
is reduced to that of
I_{k-1}
.

Case 2:
\eta_{s}\geq s-2
for all
s\in\{3,\cdots,k\}
, and
\eta_{3}=2
.

We first know
\eta_{j}\geq 2
for
j\geq 3
so that
E_{\eta_{j},j}
is independent of
x_{1}
. Since
\beta_{1}\geq 2m
always holds, we can apply Proposition
2.4
to the integral with respect to
x_{1}
whenever
\eta_{2}=0
or
\eta_{2}=1
to have

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}|r_{0}|^{-a_{0}}|r_{1}|^{-a_{1}}|E_{0,1}|^{-q_{1}}|E_{\eta_{2},2}|^{-q_{2}}{\rm d}x_{1}\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}{\rm d}x_{1}}{|x_{0}-x_{1}|^{a_{0}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{a_{1}}|E_{x_{0}x_{1}x_{1}x_{2}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{x_{\eta_{2}}x_{\eta_{2}+1}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{(n+1-4m)}},\\
\lesssim&|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{-(n+1-4m)}|x_{0}-x_{2}|^{-\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}},\end{split}

(5.84)

and consequently

\begin{split}I_{k}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)n}}&\frac{\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-\beta_{2}-}\cdots\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}}{|x_{0}-x_{2}|^{\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{a_{2}}\cdots|x_{k}-x_{k+1}|^{a_{k}}}\\
&\times\frac{{\rm d}x_{2}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}}{|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{\eta_{3},3}|^{q_{3}}\cdots|E_{\eta_{k},k}|^{q_{k}}},\end{split}

so the estimate is reduced to that of
I_{k-1}
with the obvious relabeling

\begin{gathered}y_{j}=\begin{cases}x_{0},\quad&j=0,\\
x_{j+1},&1\leq j\leq k,\end{cases}\quad\tilde{a}_{j}=\begin{cases}\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\},\quad&j=0,\\
a_{j+1},&1\leq j\leq k-1,\end{cases}\\
\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\begin{cases}0,\quad&j=1,\\
\eta_{j+1},&2\leq j\leq k-1,\end{cases}\quad(\tilde{\beta}_{j},\tilde{q}_{j})=(\beta_{j+1},q_{j+1}),\quad 1\leq j\leq k-1,\end{gathered}

and an easy check of relevant conditions in (
5.79
) where we remark that

\tilde{a}_{0}+\tilde{a}_{k-1}=\min\{a_{0}+a_{k},a_{1}+a_{k}\}\geq\min\{a_{0}+a_{k},n-2m\}\geq\max\{\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}$},q_{k}\}=\max\{\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}$},\tilde{q}_{k-1}\}.

Case 3:
\eta_{s}\geq s-2
for all
s\in\{3,\cdots,k\}
, and
\eta_{3}=1
.

We must have
\{E_{\eta_{1},1},E_{\eta_{2},2},E_{\eta_{3},3}\}=\{E_{0,1},E_{1,2},E_{1,3}\}
. Since
E_{1,3}=E_{1,2}+E_{2,3}
, it follows from homogeneity argument that

|E_{1,2}|^{-(n+1-4m)}|E_{1,3}|^{-q_{3}}\lesssim|E_{1,2}|^{-(n+1-4m)}|E_{2,3}|^{-q_{3}}+|E_{2,3}|^{-(n+1-4m)}|E_{1,3}|^{-q_{3}},

and then

\begin{split}I_{k}\lesssim&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}\cdots\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}}{|r_{0}|^{a_{0}}\cdots|r_{k}|^{a_{k}}|E_{0,1}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{1,2}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{2,3}|^{q_{3}}\cdots|E_{\eta_{k},k}|^{q_{k}}}\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{kn}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}\cdots\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}}{|r_{0}|^{a_{0}}\cdots|r_{k}|^{a_{k}}|E_{0,1}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{2,3}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{1,3}|^{q_{3}}\cdots|E_{\eta_{k},k}|^{q_{k}}}\\
:=&I_{k}^{1}+I_{k}^{2}.\end{split}

Bounding
I_{k}^{1}
has essentially been discussed, because the integral with respect to
x_{1}
is exactly (
5.84
) with
\eta_{2}=1
, and all consequences follow with no change.

Therefore, we are left to bound
I_{k}^{2}
. We first apply Proposition
2.4
to the integral with respect to
x_{1}
to get

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}{\rm d}x_{1}}{|r_{0}|^{a_{0}}|r_{1}|^{a_{1}}|E_{0,1}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{1,3}|^{q_{3}}}\lesssim|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{-q_{3}}|x_{0}-x_{2}|^{-\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}},

where
\beta_{1}\geq 2m
is used. If
k=3
, then

\begin{split}I_{k}^{2}=I_{3}^{2}\lesssim&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\frac{\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-\beta_{2}-}\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-\beta_{3}-}{\rm d}x_{2}{\rm d}x_{3}}{|x_{0}-x_{2}|^{\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{a_{2}}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{a_{3}}|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{q_{3}}|E_{x_{2}x_{3}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{n+1-4m}}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-\beta_{2}-}{\rm d}x_{2}}{|x_{0}-x_{2}|^{\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}}|x_{2}-x_{4}|^{\min\{a_{2},a_{3}\}}|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{2}x_{4}}|^{q_{3}}}\\
\lesssim&1,\quad|x_{0}-x_{4}|=|x_{0}-x_{k+1}|\geq 1,\end{split}

where in the last step we have used the fact that

\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}+\min\{a_{2},a_{3}\}\geq\min\{a_{0}+a_{3},n-2m\}\geq\max\{\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}$},q_{3}\}.

If
k\geq 4
, we have
q_{3}=n+1-4m
and

\begin{split}I_{k}^{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)n}}&\frac{\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-\beta_{2}-}\cdots\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}}{|x_{0}-x_{2}|^{\min\{a_{0},a_{1}\}}|r_{2}|^{a_{2}}\cdots|r_{k}|^{a_{k}}}\\
&\times\frac{{\rm d}x_{2}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}}{|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{x_{2}x_{3}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{\eta_{4},4}|^{q_{4}}\cdots|E_{\eta_{k},k}|^{q_{k}}}\end{split}

(5.85)

The triangle inequality implies

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{x_{2}x_{3}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{n+1-4m}}\\
\lesssim&\frac{1}{|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{x_{2}x_{3}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{n+1-4m}}+\frac{1}{|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{x_{0}x_{2}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{n+1-4m}},\end{split}

(5.86)

and we also note that
\eta_{4}=3
must hold, because otherwise
2=\eta_{4}\leq\eta_{3}<3<4
yields the contradiction
\eta_{3}=2
. So we may apply Proposition
2.4
and (
5.86
) to the integral with respect to
x_{2}
on the RHS of (
5.85
) where
E_{\eta_{4},4},\cdots,E_{\eta_{k},k}
are irrelevant, to get

I_{k}^{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k-2)n}}\frac{\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-\beta_{3}-}\cdots\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}{\rm d}x_{3}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k}}{|x_{0}-x_{3}|^{\min\{a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\}}|r_{3}|^{a_{3}}\cdots|r_{k}|^{a_{k}}|E_{x_{0}x_{3}x_{3}x_{4}}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{\eta_{4},4}|^{q_{4}}\cdots|E_{\eta_{k},k}|^{q_{k}}},

then the estimate is reduced to that of
I_{k-2}
with the relabeling

\begin{gathered}y_{j}=\begin{cases}x_{0},\quad&j=0,\\
x_{j+2},&1\leq j\leq k-1,\end{cases}\quad\tilde{a}_{j}=\begin{cases}\min\{a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\},\quad&j=0,\\
a_{j+2},&1\leq j\leq k-2,\end{cases}\\
\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\begin{cases}0,\quad&j=1,\\
\eta_{j+2}&2\leq j\leq k-2,\end{cases}\quad(\tilde{\beta}_{j},\tilde{q}_{j})=(\beta_{j+2},q_{j+2}),\quad 1\leq j\leq k-2,\end{gathered}

and the proof is complete with an easy check of relevant conditions in (
5.79
).
∎

Lemma 5.17
.

Suppose
n\geq 4m+1
,
k\geq 2
,
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
is admissible in (
5.72
),
j_{0}\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{k\}
be fixed with
j_{0}\leq\eta_{j}\,(j_{0}+1\leq j\leq k)
,
q_{i}=n+1-4m
for
i\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{j_{0}\}
,
q_{j_{0}}=\frac{n+1}{2}-2m
,
n-2m\leq a_{i}\leq n-2\,(i\notin\{0,k\})
,
0\leq a_{0},a_{k}\leq n-2
and either

a_{0},a_{k}\geq\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}$},\,\,\beta_{i}\geq 2m\,(i\in\mathbb{K}),

(5.87)

or

a_{0}+a_{k}\geq\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}$},\,\,\beta_{i}\geq\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}$}\,(i\in\mathbb{K}).

(5.88)

Then

I_{k}(x_{0},x_{k+1};\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{k},a_{0},\cdots,a_{k},q_{1},\cdots,q_{k})\lesssim 1,\quad|x_{0}-x_{k+1}|\gtrsim 1.

Proof.

We only show the proof when (
5.87
) holds, for the other case when (
5.88
) holds can be shown in a parallel way.

If
k=2
, then
j_{0}=\eta_{2}=1
and
\{E_{\eta_{1},1},E_{\eta_{2},2}\}=\{E_{0,1},E_{1,2}\}
. One checks with Proposition
2.4
to deduce

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-\beta_{2}-}|r_{1}|^{-a_{1}}|r_{2}|^{-a_{2}}|E_{0,1}|^{-q_{1}}|E_{1,2}|^{-q_{2}}{\rm d}x_{2}\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-\beta_{2}-}{\rm d}x_{2}}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{a_{1}}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{a_{2}}|E_{x_{0}x_{1}x_{1}x_{2}}|^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}|E_{x_{1}x_{2}x_{2}x_{3}}|^{n+1-4m}},\\
\lesssim&|E_{x_{0}x_{1}x_{1}x_{3}}|^{-(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}|x_{1}-x_{3}|^{-\min\{a_{1},a_{2}\}},\quad|x_{1}-x_{3}|>0,\end{split}

and then

I_{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}{\rm d}x_{1}}{|x_{0}-x_{1}|^{a_{0}}|x_{1}-x_{3}|^{\min\{a_{1},a_{2}\}}|E_{x_{0}x_{1}x_{1}x_{3}}|^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}}\lesssim 1,\quad|x_{0}-x_{3}|\gtrsim 1,

where we have used
a_{1}\geq n-2m
to make sure
\min\{a_{1},a_{2}\}+a_{0}\geq\frac{n-1}{2}
.

If
k\geq 3
, by the second assertion of Proposition
5.15
, we can find
\tau\in\mathbb{K}
such that
x_{\tau}
is independent of
E_{\eta_{j},j}
unless
j\in\{\tau-1,\tau\}
, satisfying either

j_{0}\leq\eta_{k}\leq\eta_{\tau+1}<\eta_{\tau}<\tau<\tau+1\leq k,

or

j_{0}\leq\eta_{k}<\tau=k.

We split the argument into two cases.

Case 1:
j_{0}=\tau-1
.

We must have
j_{0}=\eta_{k}=k-1
in this case. We apply Proposition
2.4
to the integral with respect to
x_{k}
and get

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\langle x_{k}\rangle^{-\beta_{k}-}{\rm d}x_{k}}{|x_{k-1}-x_{k}|^{a_{k-1}}|x_{k}-x_{k+1}|^{a_{k}}|E_{x_{\eta_{k-1}}x_{\eta_{k-1}+1}x_{k-1}x_{k}}|^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}|E_{x_{k-1}x_{k}x_{k}x_{k+1}}|^{n+1-4m}}\\
&\lesssim|E_{x_{\eta_{k-1}}x_{\eta_{k-1}+1}x_{k-1}x_{k+1}}|^{-(\frac{n+1}{2}-2m)}|x_{k-1}-x_{k+1}|^{-\min\{a_{k-1},a_{k}\}},\quad|x_{k-1}-x_{k+1}|>0,\end{split}

where we have used

a_{k-1}+a_{k}+\beta_{k}-n\geq a_{k},\quad a_{k-1}+a_{k}-(n+1-4m)\geq a_{k}.

Consequently

\begin{split}I_{k}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)n}}&\frac{\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\beta_{1}-}\cdots\langle x_{k-1}\rangle^{-\beta_{k-1}-}}{|x_{0}-x_{1}|^{a_{0}}\cdots|x_{k-2}-x_{k-1}|^{a_{k-2}}|x_{k-1}-x_{k+1}|^{\min\{a_{k-1},a_{k}\}}}\\
&\times\frac{{\rm d}x_{1}\cdots{\rm d}x_{k-1}}{|E_{\eta_{1},1}|^{n+1-4m}\cdots|E_{\eta_{k-2},k-2}|^{n+1-4m}|E_{x_{\eta_{k-1}}x_{\eta_{k-1}+1}x_{k-1}x_{k+1}}|^{\frac{n+1}{2}-2m}}.\end{split}

The desired estimate is then implied by Proposition
5.16
, where in the relevant conditions (
5.79
) one mainly checks by
a_{k-1}\geq n-2m
that

\min\{a_{0}+\min\{a_{k-1},a_{k}\},n+1-4m\}\geq\min\{\mbox{$\frac{n-1}{2}$},n+1-4m\}\geq\mbox{$\frac{n+1}{2}$}-2m.

Case 2:
j_{0}<\tau-1
.

Since
j_{0}<j_{0}+1<\tau
in this case, we have
q_{j_{0}+1}=\cdots=q_{\tau}=\cdots=q_{k}=n+1-4m
. The same type of argument from (
5.76
) to (
5.78
) shows that
E_{\eta_{j},j}
is irrelevant in the integral with respect to
x_{\tau}
if
j\notin\{\tau-1,\tau\}
, which is exactly (
5.80
), and therefore the application of Proposition
2.4
gives

\begin{split}I_{k}\lesssim&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)n}}\frac{\prod\limits_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{\tau\}}\langle x_{j}\rangle^{-\beta_{\tau}-}}{|x_{\tau-1}-x_{\tau+1}|^{\min\{a_{\tau-1},a_{\tau}\}}|E_{x_{\eta_{\tau-1}}x_{\eta_{\tau-1}+1}x_{\tau-1}x_{\tau+1}}|^{n+1-4m}}\\
&\quad\quad\times\frac{\prod\limits_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{\tau\}}{\rm d}x_{j}}{\prod\limits_{j\in\mathbb{K}_{0}\setminus\{\tau-1,\tau\}}|r_{j}|^{a_{j}}\prod\limits_{j\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{\tau-1,\tau\}}|E_{x_{\eta_{j}}x_{\eta_{j}+1}x_{j}x_{j+1}}|^{q_{j}}}.\end{split}

If
\tau=k
, then
\min\{a_{\tau-1},a_{\tau}\}\geq\frac{n-1}{2}
. If
\tau<k
, one then has
1<\tau<k
and
\min\{a_{\tau-1},a_{\tau}\}\geq n+1-4m
by the assumptions in this lemma. Therefore the estimate can be immediately reduced to that of
I_{k-2}
, and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 5.18
.

Suppose
n\geq 4m+1
,
k\geq 2
,
\{(\eta_{j},j);\,j\in\mathbb{K}\}
is admissible in (
5.72
), and

\begin{cases}n-2m\leq a_{i}\leq n-2\,(a\leq i\leq k-1),\,\,0\leq a_{0},a_{k}\leq n-2,\\
\beta_{i}\geq 2m\,(i\in\mathbb{K}),\\
q_{i}=n+1-4m\,\,(i\in\mathbb{K}).\end{cases}

1)

If
n-2\geq a_{0}+a_{k}\geq\frac{n-1}{2}
, then

I_{k}(x_{0},x_{k+1};\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{k},a_{0},\cdots,a_{k},q_{1},\cdots,q_{k})\lesssim 1,\quad 0<|x_{0}-x_{k+1}|\lesssim 1.

2)

If
i_{0}\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{k\}
and
